# Space-time error estimates for deep neural network approximations for differential equations

Philipp Grohs¹, Fabian Hornung^(2,3),\
Arnulf Jentzen⁴, and Philipp Zimmermann^(5,6)\
\
¹Faculty of Mathematics and Research Platform Data Science, University of Vienna\
Austria, e-mail: philipp.grohs@univie.ac.at\
² Department of Mathematics, ETH Zurich, Zürich,\
Switzerland, e-mail: fabianhornung89@gmail.com\
³ Faculty of Mathematics, Karlsruhe Institute of Technology, Karlsruhe,\
Germany, e-mail: fabianhornung89@gmail.com\
⁴ Department of Mathematics, ETH Zurich, Zürich,\
Switzerland, e-mail: arnulf.jentzen@sam.math.ethz.ch\
⁵ Department of Mathematics, ETH Zurich, Zürich,\
Switzerland, e-mail: philipp.zimmermann@math.ethz.ch\
⁶ Faculty of Mathematics, University of Regensburg, Regensburg,\
Germany, e-mail: philipp.zimmermann@ur.de

###### Abstract

Over the last few years deep artificial neural networks (DNNs) have very successfully been used in numerical simulations for a wide variety of computational problems including computer vision, image classification, speech recognition, natural language processing, as well as computational advertisement. In addition, it has recently been proposed to approximate solutions of partial differential equations (PDEs) by means of stochastic learning problems involving DNNs. There are now also a few rigorous mathematical results in the scientific literature which provide error estimates for such deep learning based approximation methods for PDEs. All of these articles provide spatial error estimates for neural network approximations for PDEs but do not provide error estimates for the entire space-time error for the considered neural network approximations. It is the subject of the main result of this article to provide space-time error estimates for DNN approximations of Euler approximations of certain perturbed differential equations. Our proof of this result is based (i) on a certain artificial neural network (ANN) calculus and (ii) on ANN approximation results for products of the form $`{{\lbrack 0,T\rbrack} \times {\mathbb{R}}^{d}} \ni {(t,x)}\mapsto{t\hspace{0pt}x} \in {\mathbb{R}}^{d}`$ where $`T \in {(0,\infty)}`$, $`d \in {\mathbb{N}}`$, which we both develop within this article.

## 1 Introduction

Over the last few years deep artificial neural networks (DNNs) have very successfully been used in numerical simulations for a wide variety of computational problems including computer vision, image classification, speech recognition, natural language processing, as well as computational advertisement (cf., e.g., the references mentioned in \[14, 17, 25\]). In addition, the articles \[9, 18\] suggest to approximate solutions of partial differential equations (PDEs) by means of stochastic learning problems involving DNNs. We also refer to \[1, 2, 3, 4, 5, 6, 8, 10, 12, 13, 15, 19, 20, 21, 24, 28, 29, 30, 33, 34, 36\] for extensions and improvements of such deep learning based approximation methods for PDEs. There are now also a few rigorous mathematical results in the scientific literature which provide error estimates for such deep learning based approximation methods for PDEs; see, e.g., \[7, 11, 16, 19, 22, 25, 26, 35, 36\]. The articles in this reference list all provide spatial error estimates for neural network approximations for PDEs but do not provide error estimates for the entire space-time error for the considered neural network approximations. It is the subject of Theorem 3.12 in this article, which is the main result of this article, to provide space-time error estimates for DNN approximations of Euler approximations of certain perturbed differential equations. To illustrate the findings of the main result of this article in more details, we now formulate in Theorem 1.1 below a special case of Theorem 3.12.

###### Theorem 1.1.

Let $`{{\mathfrak{C}},T,{\mathfrak{d}}} \in {(0,\infty)}`$, let $`A_{d} \in {C\hspace{0pt}{({\mathbb{R}}^{d},{\mathbb{R}}^{d})}}`$, $`d \in {\mathbb{N}}`$, satisfy for all $`d \in {\mathbb{N}}`$, $`x = {(x_{1},x_{2},\ldots,x_{d})} \in {\mathbb{R}}^{d}`$ that $`{A_{d}\hspace{0pt}{(x)}} = {({\max{\{ x_{1},0\}}},{\max{\{ x_{2},0\}}},\ldots,{\max{\{ x_{d},0\}}})}`$, let $`\mathbf{N} = \cup_{L \in {\mathbb{N}}} \cup_{{(l_{0},l_{1},\ldots,l_{L})} \in {\mathbb{N}}^{L + 1}}\left( \times_{k = 1}^{L}{({\mathbb{R}}^{l_{k} \times l_{k - 1}} \times {\mathbb{R}}^{l_{k}})} \right)`$, let $`R:{\mathbf{N}\rightarrow{\cup_{{k,l} \in {\mathbb{N}}}{C\hspace{0pt}{({\mathbb{R}}^{k},{\mathbb{R}}^{l})}}}}`$ and $`P:{\mathbf{N}\rightarrow{\mathbb{N}}}`$ satisfy for all $`L \in {\mathbb{N}}`$, $`{l_{0},l_{1},\ldots,l_{L}} \in {\mathbb{N}}`$, $`\Phi \in {( \times_{k = 1}^{L}{({\mathbb{R}}^{l_{k} \times l_{k - 1}} \times {\mathbb{R}}^{l_{k}})})}`$, $`\Psi = {({(W_{1},B_{1})},{(W_{2},B_{2})},\ldots,{(W_{L},B_{L})})} \in {( \times_{k = 1}^{L}{({\mathbb{R}}^{l_{k} \times l_{k - 1}} \times {\mathbb{R}}^{l_{k}})})}`$, $`{x_{0} \in {\mathbb{R}}^{l_{0}}},{{x_{1} \in {{\mathbb{R}}^{l_{1}},\ldots}},{x_{L - 1} \in {\mathbb{R}}^{l_{L - 1}}}}`$ with $`{{\forall k} \in {{\mathbb{N}} \cap {(0,L)}}}:{x_{k} = {A_{l_{k}}\hspace{0pt}{({{W_{k}\hspace{0pt}x_{k - 1}} + B_{k}})}}}`$ that $`{P\hspace{0pt}{(\Phi)}} = {\sum_{k = 1}^{L}{l_{k}\hspace{0pt}{({l_{k - 1} + 1})}}}`$, $`{{R\hspace{0pt}{(\Psi)}} \in {C\hspace{0pt}{({\mathbb{R}}^{l_{0}},{\mathbb{R}}^{l_{L}})}}},{{\text{~and~}\hspace{0pt}{({R\hspace{0pt}{(\Psi)}})}\hspace{0pt}{(x_{0})}} = {{W_{L}\hspace{0pt}x_{L - 1}} + B_{L}}}`$, let $`\Phi_{d} \in \mathbf{N}`$, $`d \in {\mathbb{N}}`$, satisfy for all $`d \in {\mathbb{N}}`$, $`x \in {\mathbb{R}}^{d}`$ that $`{R\hspace{0pt}{(\Phi_{d})}} \in {C\hspace{0pt}{({\mathbb{R}}^{d},{\mathbb{R}}^{d})}}`$, $`{\|{{({R\hspace{0pt}{(\Phi_{d})}})}\hspace{0pt}{(x)}}\|} \leq {{\mathfrak{C}}\hspace{0pt}{({1 + {\| x\|}})}}`$, and $`{P\hspace{0pt}{(\Phi_{d})}} \leq {{\mathfrak{C}}\hspace{0pt}d^{\mathfrak{d}}}`$, let $`{Y^{d,N} = {(Y_{t,x}^{d,N})}_{{(t,x)} \in {{\lbrack 0,T\rbrack} \times {\mathbb{R}}^{d}}}}:{{{\lbrack 0,T\rbrack} \times {\mathbb{R}}^{d}}\rightarrow{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}`$, $`{N,d} \in {\mathbb{N}}`$, be the functions which satisfy for all $`{d,N} \in {\mathbb{N}}`$, $`n \in {\{ 0,1,\ldots,{N - 1}\}}`$, $`t \in \left\lbrack \frac{n\hspace{0pt}T}{N},\frac{{({n + 1})}\hspace{0pt}T}{N} \right\rbrack`$, $`x \in {\mathbb{R}}^{d}`$ that $`Y_{0,x}^{d,N} = x`$ and

```math
\begin{array}{cl}
 & {{Y_{t,x}^{d,N} = {Y_{\frac{n\hspace{0pt}T}{N},x}^{d,N} + {\left( {t - \frac{n\hspace{0pt}T}{N}} \right)\hspace{0pt}{({R\hspace{0pt}{(\Phi_{d})}})}\hspace{0pt}{(Y_{\frac{n\hspace{0pt}T}{N},x}^{d,N})}}}}.}
\end{array}
```

Then there exist $`C \in {\mathbb{R}}`$ and $`\Psi_{\varepsilon,d,N} \in \mathbf{N}`$, $`{N,d} \in {\mathbb{N}}`$, $`\varepsilon \in {(0,1\rbrack}`$, such that

1.  (i)
    it holds for all $`\varepsilon \in {(0,1\rbrack}`$, $`{d,N} \in {\mathbb{N}}`$ that $`{R\hspace{0pt}{(\Psi_{\varepsilon,d,N})}} \in {C\hspace{0pt}{({\mathbb{R}}^{d + 1},{\mathbb{R}}^{d})}}`$,
2.  (ii)
    it holds for all $`\varepsilon \in {(0,1\rbrack}`$, $`{d,N} \in {\mathbb{N}}`$, $`t \in {\lbrack 0,T\rbrack}`$, $`x \in {\mathbb{R}}^{d}`$ that

    ```math
    {{\|{Y_{t,x}^{d,N} - {{({R\hspace{0pt}{(\Psi_{\varepsilon,d,N})}})}\hspace{0pt}{(t,x)}}}\|} \leq {C\hspace{0pt}d^{1/2}\hspace{0pt}N^{3/2}\hspace{0pt}\varepsilon\hspace{0pt}{({1 + {\| x\|}^{3}})}}},
    ```

3.  (iii)
    it holds for all $`\varepsilon \in {(0,1\rbrack}`$, $`{d,N} \in {\mathbb{N}}`$, $`t \in {\lbrack 0,T\rbrack}`$, $`x \in {\mathbb{R}}^{d}`$ that

    ```math
    {{\|{{({R\hspace{0pt}{(\Psi_{\varepsilon,d,N})}})}\hspace{0pt}{(t,x)}}\|} \leq {C\hspace{0pt}d^{1/2}\hspace{0pt}N\hspace{0pt}{({1 + {\| x\|}^{2}})}}},
    ```

    and

4.  (iv)
    it holds for all $`\varepsilon \in {(0,1\rbrack}`$, $`{d,N} \in {\mathbb{N}}`$ that

    ```math
    {{P\hspace{0pt}{(\Psi_{\varepsilon,d,N})}} \leq {C\hspace{0pt}d^{16 + {8\hspace{0pt}{\mathfrak{d}}}}\hspace{0pt}N^{6}\hspace{0pt}\left\lbrack {1 + {|{\ln{(\varepsilon)}}|}^{2}} \right\rbrack}}.
    ```

Theorem 1.1 is an immediate consequence of Corollary 3.13 in Subsection 3.3.5 below. Corollary 3.13, in turn, follows from Theorem 3.12 in Subsection 3.3.5, which is the main result of this article. Our proof of Theorem 1.1 and Theorem 3.12, respectively, is based on a certain artificial neural network (ANN) calculus, which we develop in Section 2 calculus ‣ Space-time error estimates for deep neural network approximations for differential equations"). Section 2 calculus ‣ Space-time error estimates for deep neural network approximations for differential equations") is in parts based on several well-known concepts and results in the scientific literature (cf., e.g., \[11, 25, 32, 37\]). We refer to the beginning of Section 2 calculus ‣ Space-time error estimates for deep neural network approximations for differential equations") for a more detailed comparison of the content of Section 2 calculus ‣ Space-time error estimates for deep neural network approximations for differential equations") with the material in related articles in the scientific literature. Our proof of Theorem 1.1 and Theorem 3.12, respectively, is mainly inspired by \[25\], \[11, Section 6\], and \[37, Section 3.2\]. Theorem 1.1 and Theorem 3.12, respectively, provide error estimates for rectified DNN approximations of Euler approximations of certain perturbed differential equations. Many of the DNN approximation and representation results of this work, however, apply to DNNs with more general activation functions than only the rectifier function (cf., e.g., Li et al. \[27, Section 1\] and Petersen et al. \[31, Section 2\] for further activation functions). The error estimates for rectified DNN approximations of Euler approximations of perturbed differential equations, which we establish in Theorem 1.1 and Theorem 3.12, respectively, can then be used to establish space-time error estimates for DNN approximations for PDEs. This will be the subject of a future research article, which will be based on this article.

The remainder of this article is organized as follows. In Section 2 calculus ‣ Space-time error estimates for deep neural network approximations for differential equations") we develop the above mentioned ANN calculus and, in particular, we establish in Subsection 2.5 calculus ‣ Space-time error estimates for deep neural network approximations for differential equations") ANN representation results for Euler approximations. In Subsection 3.1 we develop ANN approximation results for the square function $`{\mathbb{R}} \ni x\mapsto x^{2} \in {\mathbb{R}}`$. These ANN approximation results for the square function are then used in Subsection 3.2 to develop ANN approximation results for products of the form $`{{\lbrack 0,T\rbrack} \times {\mathbb{R}}^{d}} \ni {(t,x)}\mapsto{t\hspace{0pt}x} \in {\mathbb{R}}^{d}`$ where $`T \in {(0,\infty)}`$, $`d \in {\mathbb{N}}`$. In Subsection 3.3 we then combine the ANN representation results in Subsection 2.5 calculus ‣ Space-time error estimates for deep neural network approximations for differential equations") with the ANN approximation results for products in Subsection 3.2 to establish in Theorem 3.12 the main result of this article.

## 2 Artificial neural network (ANN) calculus

This section develops a certain calculus for ANNs. Some of the notions and results which we present here are rather elementary, but for convenience of the reader we present here all details and we include the proof of every result. The material in this section is also in parts based on several well-known concepts and results in the scientific literature. In particular, Definition 2.1). ‣ 2.1 Artificial neural networks (ANNs) and their realizations ‣ 2 Artificial neural network (ANN) calculus ‣ Space-time error estimates for deep neural network approximations for differential equations"), Definition 2.2. ‣ 2.1 Artificial neural networks (ANNs) and their realizations ‣ 2 Artificial neural network (ANN) calculus ‣ Space-time error estimates for deep neural network approximations for differential equations"), and Definition 2.3. ‣ 2.1 Artificial neural networks (ANNs) and their realizations ‣ 2 Artificial neural network (ANN) calculus ‣ Space-time error estimates for deep neural network approximations for differential equations") are slight reformulations of Petersen & Voigtlaender \[32, Definition 2.1\]. Moreover, Lemma 2.4 and their realizations ‣ 2 Artificial neural network (ANN) calculus ‣ Space-time error estimates for deep neural network approximations for differential equations") is elementary and well-known in the scientific literature. Furthermore, Definition 2.5. ‣ 2.2.1 Standard compositions of ANNs ‣ 2.2 Compositions of ANNs ‣ 2 Artificial neural network (ANN) calculus ‣ Space-time error estimates for deep neural network approximations for differential equations") is also a slight reformulation of Petersen & Voigtlaender \[32, Definition 2.2\]. In addition, Proposition 2.6 calculus ‣ Space-time error estimates for deep neural network approximations for differential equations"), Corollary 2.7 calculus ‣ Space-time error estimates for deep neural network approximations for differential equations"), and Lemma 2.8 calculus ‣ Space-time error estimates for deep neural network approximations for differential equations") are elementary and essentially well-known in the scientific literature (cf., e.g., Petersen & Voigtlaender \[32\]). Moreover, Definition 2.11 calculus ‣ Space-time error estimates for deep neural network approximations for differential equations") is an extension of Elbrächter et al. \[11, Setting 5.2\] and Proposition 2.16 calculus ‣ Space-time error estimates for deep neural network approximations for differential equations") is in parts an extension of Elbrächter et al. \[11, Lemma 5.3\]. Furthermore, Definition 2.17. ‣ 2.3.1 Parallelizations of ANNs with the same length ‣ 2.3 Parallelizations of ANNs ‣ 2 Artificial neural network (ANN) calculus ‣ Space-time error estimates for deep neural network approximations for differential equations") and Definition 2.22. ‣ 2.3.2 Parallelizations of ANNs with different lengths ‣ 2.3 Parallelizations of ANNs ‣ 2 Artificial neural network (ANN) calculus ‣ Space-time error estimates for deep neural network approximations for differential equations") extend Elbrächter et al. \[11, Setting 5.2\] (cf., e.g., Petersen & Voigtlaender \[32, Definition 2.7\]). In addition, Proposition 2.25 calculus ‣ Space-time error estimates for deep neural network approximations for differential equations") is a reformulation of \[25, Lemma 5.1\]. Moreover, Lemma 2.27 calculus ‣ Space-time error estimates for deep neural network approximations for differential equations") and Proposition 2.28 calculus ‣ Space-time error estimates for deep neural network approximations for differential equations") are significantly inspired by \[25, Proposition 5.3\]. Furthermore, item (iv calculus ‣ Space-time error estimates for deep neural network approximations for differential equations")) in Lemma 2.27 calculus ‣ Space-time error estimates for deep neural network approximations for differential equations") and item (iv calculus ‣ Space-time error estimates for deep neural network approximations for differential equations")) in Proposition 2.28 calculus ‣ Space-time error estimates for deep neural network approximations for differential equations"), respectively, improve the parameter estimates in \[25, Proposition 5.3\]. In addition, Corollary 2.31 calculus ‣ Space-time error estimates for deep neural network approximations for differential equations") in Subsection 2.5.2 calculus ‣ Space-time error estimates for deep neural network approximations for differential equations") below is also in parts inspired by \[25, Proposition 6.1\].

### 2.1 Artificial neural networks (ANNs) and their realizations

###### Definition 2.1 (Artificial neural networks (ANNs)).

We denote by $`\mathbf{N}`$ the set given by

```math
\begin{aligned}
\mathbf{N} & {= \cup_{L \in {\mathbb{N}}} \cup_{{(l_{0},l_{1},\ldots,l_{L})} \in {\mathbb{N}}^{L + 1}}\left( \times_{k = 1}^{L}{({\mathbb{R}}^{l_{k} \times l_{k - 1}} \times {\mathbb{R}}^{l_{k}})} \right)}
\end{aligned}
```

and we denote by $`{\mathcal{P},\mathcal{L},\mathcal{I},\mathcal{O}}:{\mathbf{N}\rightarrow{\mathbb{N}}}`$, $`\mathcal{H}:{\mathbf{N}\rightarrow{\mathbb{N}}_{0}}`$, and $`\mathcal{D}:{\mathbf{N}\rightarrow{\cup_{L = 2}^{\infty}{\mathbb{N}}^{L}}}`$ the functions which satisfy for all $`L \in {\mathbb{N}}`$, $`{l_{0},l_{1},\ldots,l_{L}} \in {\mathbb{N}}`$, $`\Phi \in {( \times_{k = 1}^{L}{({\mathbb{R}}^{l_{k} \times l_{k - 1}} \times {\mathbb{R}}^{l_{k}})})}`$ that $`{\mathcal{P}\hspace{0pt}{(\Phi)}} = {\sum_{k = 1}^{L}{l_{k}\hspace{0pt}{({l_{k - 1} + 1})}}}`$, $`{\mathcal{L}\hspace{0pt}{(\Phi)}} = L`$, $`{\mathcal{I}\hspace{0pt}{(\Phi)}} = l_{0}`$, $`{\mathcal{O}\hspace{0pt}{(\Phi)}} = l_{L}`$, $`{\mathcal{H}\hspace{0pt}{(\Phi)}} = {L - 1}`$, and $`{\mathcal{D}\hspace{0pt}{(\Phi)}} = {(l_{0},l_{1},\ldots,l_{L})}`$.

###### Definition 2.2 (Multidimensional versions).

Let $`d \in {\mathbb{N}}`$ and let $`\psi:{{\mathbb{R}}\rightarrow{\mathbb{R}}}`$ be a function. Then we denote by $`{\mathfrak{M}}_{\psi,d}:{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}\rightarrow{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}`$ the function which satisfies for all $`x = {(x_{1},\ldots,x_{d})} \in {\mathbb{R}}^{d}`$ that

```math
{{{\mathfrak{M}}_{\psi,d}\hspace{0pt}{(x)}} = \left( {\psi\hspace{0pt}{(x_{1})}},\ldots,{\psi\hspace{0pt}{(x_{d})}} \right)}.
```

###### Definition 2.3 (Realizations associated to ANNs).

Let $`a \in {C\hspace{0pt}{({\mathbb{R}},{\mathbb{R}})}}`$. Then we denote by $`\mathcal{R}_{a}:{\mathbf{N}\rightarrow{\cup_{{k,l} \in {\mathbb{N}}}{C\hspace{0pt}{({\mathbb{R}}^{k},{\mathbb{R}}^{l})}}}}`$ the function which satisfies for all $`L \in {\mathbb{N}}`$, $`{l_{0},l_{1},\ldots,l_{L}} \in {\mathbb{N}}`$, $`\Phi = {({(W_{1},B_{1})},{(W_{2},B_{2})},\ldots,{(W_{L},B_{L})})} \in {( \times_{k = 1}^{L}{({\mathbb{R}}^{l_{k} \times l_{k - 1}} \times {\mathbb{R}}^{l_{k}})})}`$, $`{x_{0} \in {\mathbb{R}}^{l_{0}}},{{x_{1} \in {{\mathbb{R}}^{l_{1}},\ldots}},{x_{L - 1} \in {\mathbb{R}}^{l_{L - 1}}}}`$ with $`{{\forall k} \in {{\mathbb{N}} \cap {(0,L)}}}:{x_{k} = {{\mathfrak{M}}_{a,l_{k}}\hspace{0pt}{({{W_{k}\hspace{0pt}x_{k - 1}} + B_{k}})}}}`$ that

```math
{{\mathcal{R}_{a}\hspace{0pt}{(\Phi)}} \in {{C\hspace{0pt}{({\mathbb{R}}^{l_{0}},{\mathbb{R}}^{l_{L}})}}\qquad\text{and}}}\qquad{{{({\mathcal{R}_{a}\hspace{0pt}{(\Phi)}})}\hspace{0pt}{(x_{0})}} = {{W_{L}\hspace{0pt}x_{L - 1}} + B_{L}}}
```

(cf. Definition 2.2. ‣ 2.1 Artificial neural networks (ANNs) and their realizations ‣ 2 Artificial neural network (ANN) calculus ‣ Space-time error estimates for deep neural network approximations for differential equations") and Definition 2.1). ‣ 2.1 Artificial neural networks (ANNs) and their realizations ‣ 2 Artificial neural network (ANN) calculus ‣ Space-time error estimates for deep neural network approximations for differential equations")).

###### Lemma 2.4.

Let $`\Phi \in \mathbf{N}`$ (cf. Definition 2.1). ‣ 2.1 Artificial neural networks (ANNs) and their realizations ‣ 2 Artificial neural network (ANN) calculus ‣ Space-time error estimates for deep neural network approximations for differential equations")). Then

1.  (i)
    it holds that $`{\mathcal{D}\hspace{0pt}{(\Phi)}} \in {\mathbb{N}}^{{\mathcal{L}\hspace{0pt}{(\Phi)}} + 1}`$ and
2.  (ii)
    it holds for all $`a \in {C\hspace{0pt}{({\mathbb{R}},{\mathbb{R}})}}`$ that $`{\mathcal{R}_{a}\hspace{0pt}{(\Phi)}} \in {C\hspace{0pt}{({\mathbb{R}}^{\mathcal{I}\hspace{0pt}{(\Phi)}},{\mathbb{R}}^{\mathcal{O}\hspace{0pt}{(\Phi)}})}}`$

(cf. Definition 2.3. ‣ 2.1 Artificial neural networks (ANNs) and their realizations ‣ 2 Artificial neural network (ANN) calculus ‣ Space-time error estimates for deep neural network approximations for differential equations")).

###### Proof of Lemma 2.4 and their realizations ‣ 2 Artificial neural network (ANN) calculus ‣ Space-time error estimates for deep neural network approximations for differential equations").

Note that the assumption that $`\Phi \in \mathbf{N} = \cup_{L \in {\mathbb{N}}} \cup_{{(l_{0},l_{1},\ldots,l_{L})} \in {\mathbb{N}}^{L + 1}}{( \times_{k = 1}^{L}{({\mathbb{R}}^{l_{k} \times l_{k - 1}} \times {\mathbb{R}}^{l_{k}})})}`$ ensures that there exist $`L \in {\mathbb{N}}`$, $`{l_{0},l_{1},\ldots,l_{L}} \in {\mathbb{N}}`$ such that

```math
\Phi \in \left( \times_{k = 1}^{L}{({\mathbb{R}}^{l_{k} \times l_{k - 1}} \times {\mathbb{R}}^{l_{k}})} \right).
```

Observe that (8 and their realizations ‣ 2 Artificial neural network (ANN) calculus ‣ Space-time error estimates for deep neural network approximations for differential equations")) assures that

```math
{{{\mathcal{L}\hspace{0pt}{(\Phi)}} = L},{{{\mathcal{I}\hspace{0pt}{(\Phi)}} = l_{0}},{{\mathcal{O}\hspace{0pt}{(\Phi)}} = l_{L}}}},
```

```math
{{\text{and}\qquad{\mathcal{D}\hspace{0pt}{(\Phi)}}} = {(l_{0},l_{1},\ldots,l_{L})} \in {\mathbb{N}}^{L + 1} = {\mathbb{N}}^{{\mathcal{L}\hspace{0pt}{(\Phi)}} + 1}}.
```

This establishes item (i and their realizations ‣ 2 Artificial neural network (ANN) calculus ‣ Space-time error estimates for deep neural network approximations for differential equations")). Moreover, note that (9 and their realizations ‣ 2 Artificial neural network (ANN) calculus ‣ Space-time error estimates for deep neural network approximations for differential equations")) and (7. ‣ 2.1 Artificial neural networks (ANNs) and their realizations ‣ 2 Artificial neural network (ANN) calculus ‣ Space-time error estimates for deep neural network approximations for differential equations")) show that $`{\mathcal{R}_{a}\hspace{0pt}{(\Phi)}} \in {C\hspace{0pt}{({\mathbb{R}}^{\mathcal{I}\hspace{0pt}{(\Phi)}},{\mathbb{R}}^{\mathcal{O}\hspace{0pt}{(\Phi)}})}}`$. This establishes item (ii and their realizations ‣ 2 Artificial neural network (ANN) calculus ‣ Space-time error estimates for deep neural network approximations for differential equations")). The proof of Lemma 2.4 and their realizations ‣ 2 Artificial neural network (ANN) calculus ‣ Space-time error estimates for deep neural network approximations for differential equations") is thus completed. ∎

### 2.2 Compositions of ANNs

#### 2.2.1 Standard compositions of ANNs

###### Definition 2.5 (Standard compositions of ANNs).

We denote by $`{{( \cdot )} \bullet {( \cdot )}}:{{\{{{(\Phi_{1},\Phi_{2})} \in {\mathbf{N} \times \mathbf{N}}}:{{\mathcal{I}\hspace{0pt}{(\Phi_{1})}} = {\mathcal{O}\hspace{0pt}{(\Phi_{2})}}}\}}\rightarrow\mathbf{N}}`$ the function which satisfies for all $`{L,{\mathfrak{L}}} \in {\mathbb{N}}`$, $`{l_{0},l_{1},\ldots,l_{L},{\mathfrak{l}}_{0},{\mathfrak{l}}_{1},\ldots,{\mathfrak{l}}_{\mathfrak{L}}} \in {\mathbb{N}}`$, $`\Phi_{1} = {({(W_{1},B_{1})},{(W_{2},B_{2})},\ldots,{(W_{L},B_{L})})} \in {( \times_{k = 1}^{L}{({\mathbb{R}}^{l_{k} \times l_{k - 1}} \times {\mathbb{R}}^{l_{k}})})}`$, $`\Phi_{2} = {({(\mathcal{W}_{1},{\mathfrak{B}}_{1})},{(\mathcal{W}_{2},{\mathfrak{B}}_{2})},\ldots,{(\mathcal{W}_{\mathfrak{L}},{\mathfrak{B}}_{\mathfrak{L}})})} \in {( \times_{k = 1}^{\mathfrak{L}}{({\mathbb{R}}^{{\mathfrak{l}}_{k} \times {\mathfrak{l}}_{k - 1}} \times {\mathbb{R}}^{{\mathfrak{l}}_{k}})})}`$ with $`l_{0} = {\mathcal{I}\hspace{0pt}{(\Phi_{1})}} = {\mathcal{O}\hspace{0pt}{(\Phi_{2})}} = {\mathfrak{l}}_{\mathfrak{L}}`$ that

```math
\begin{array}{cl}
 & {{\Phi_{1} \bullet \Phi_{2}} =} \\
 & \begin{cases}
\begin{array}{r}
\left( {(\mathcal{W}_{1},{\mathfrak{B}}_{1})},{(\mathcal{W}_{2},{\mathfrak{B}}_{2})},\ldots,{(\mathcal{W}_{{\mathfrak{L}} - 1},{\mathfrak{B}}_{{\mathfrak{L}} - 1})},{(W_{1}\mathcal{W}_{\mathfrak{L}},W_{1}{\mathfrak{B}}_{\mathfrak{L}} + B_{1})}, \right. \\
\left. {(W_{2},B_{2})},{(W_{3},B_{3})},\ldots,{(W_{L},B_{L})} \right)
\end{array} & {:{L > 1 < {\mathfrak{L}}}} \\
\left( {({W_{1}\hspace{0pt}\mathcal{W}_{1}},{{W_{1}\hspace{0pt}{\mathfrak{B}}_{1}} + B_{1}})},{(W_{2},B_{2})},{(W_{3},B_{3})},\ldots,{(W_{L},B_{L})} \right) & {:{L > 1 = {\mathfrak{L}}}} \\
\left( {(\mathcal{W}_{1},{\mathfrak{B}}_{1})},{(\mathcal{W}_{2},{\mathfrak{B}}_{2})},\ldots,{(\mathcal{W}_{{\mathfrak{L}} - 1},{\mathfrak{B}}_{{\mathfrak{L}} - 1})},{({W_{1}\hspace{0pt}\mathcal{W}_{\mathfrak{L}}},{{W_{1}\hspace{0pt}{\mathfrak{B}}_{\mathfrak{L}}} + B_{1}})} \right) & {:{L = 1 < {\mathfrak{L}}}} \\
{({W_{1}\hspace{0pt}\mathcal{W}_{1}},{{W_{1}\hspace{0pt}{\mathfrak{B}}_{1}} + B_{1}})} & {:{L = 1 = {\mathfrak{L}}}}
\end{cases}
\end{array}
```

(cf. Definition 2.1). ‣ 2.1 Artificial neural networks (ANNs) and their realizations ‣ 2 Artificial neural network (ANN) calculus ‣ Space-time error estimates for deep neural network approximations for differential equations")).

###### Proposition 2.6.

#### 2.2.2 Associativity of standard compositions of ANNs

###### Lemma 2.8.

Let $`{\Phi_{1},\Phi_{2},\Phi_{3}} \in \mathbf{N}`$ satisfy that $`{\mathcal{I}\hspace{0pt}{(\Phi_{1})}} = {\mathcal{O}\hspace{0pt}{(\Phi_{2})}}`$ and $`{\mathcal{I}\hspace{0pt}{(\Phi_{2})}} = {\mathcal{O}\hspace{0pt}{(\Phi_{3})}}`$ (cf. Definition 2.1). ‣ 2.1 Artificial neural networks (ANNs) and their realizations ‣ 2 Artificial neural network (ANN) calculus ‣ Space-time error estimates for deep neural network approximations for differential equations")).     it holds for all $`n \in {\{ 0,1,\ldots,N\}}`$, $`m \in {{\mathbb{N}}_{0} \cap {\lbrack 0,n\rbrack}}`$, $`x \in {\mathbb{R}}^{d}`$, $`{y = {(y_{1},y_{2},\ldots,y_{N})}},{z = {(z_{1},z_{2},\ldots,z_{N})} \in {({\mathbb{R}}^{d})}^{N}}`$ with $`{{\forall k} \in {{\mathbb{N}} \cap {\lbrack 0,n\rbrack}}}:{y_{k} = z_{k}}`$ that

    ```math
    {{{({\mathcal{R}_{a}\hspace{0pt}{(\Psi_{m,y})}})}\hspace{0pt}{(x)}} = {{({\mathcal{R}_{a}\hspace{0pt}{(\Psi_{m,z})}})}\hspace{0pt}{(x)}}}.
    ```

###### Proof of Proposition 2.32 calculus ‣ Space-time error estimates for deep neural network approximations for differential equations").

Throughout this proof let $`{l_{0},l_{1},\ldots,l_{\mathcal{L}\hspace{0pt}{(\Phi)}}} \in {\mathbb{N}}`$ satisfy that $`{\mathcal{D}\hspace{0pt}{(\Phi)}} = {(l_{0},l_{1},\ldots,l_{\mathcal{L}\hspace{0pt}{(\Phi)}})}`$, let $`{\mathbb{A}}_{n,b} \in {({{\mathbb{R}}^{d \times d} \times {\mathbb{R}}^{d}})} \subseteq \mathbf{N}`$, $`n \in {\{ 1,2,\ldots,N\}}`$, $`b \in {\mathbb{R}}^{d}`$, satisfy for all $`n \in {\{ 1,2,\ldots,N\}}`$, $`b \in {\mathbb{R}}^{d}`$ that

```math
{{\mathbb{A}}_{n,b} = {(A_{n},b)} \in {({{\mathbb{R}}^{d \times d} \times {\mathbb{R}}^{d}})}},
```

let $`\rho_{n,y} \in \mathbf{N}`$, $`n \in {\mathbb{N}}`$, $`y \in {({\mathbb{R}}^{d})}^{N}`$, satisfy for all $`n \in {\mathbb{N}}`$, $`y = {(y_{1},y_{2},\ldots,y_{N})} \in {({\mathbb{R}}^{d})}^{N}`$ that

```math
\rho_{n,y} = {{\mathbb{A}}_{{\min{\{ n,N\}}},y_{\min{\{ n,N\}}}} \bullet \Phi}
```

(cf. Definition 2.5. ‣ 2.2.1 Standard compositions of ANNs ‣ 2.2 Compositions of ANNs ‣ 2 Artificial neural network (ANN) calculus ‣ Space-time error estimates for deep neural network approximations for differential equations")), and let $`{\mathcal{Y}_{n} = {(\mathcal{Y}_{n}^{x,y})}_{{(x,y)} \in {{\mathbb{R}}^{d} \times {({\mathbb{R}}^{d})}^{N}}}}:{{{\mathbb{R}}^{d} \times {({\mathbb{R}}^{d})}^{N}}\rightarrow{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}`$, $`n \in {\mathbb{N}}_{0}`$, be the functions which satisfy for all $`n \in {\mathbb{N}}_{0}`$, $`x \in {\mathbb{R}}^{d}`$, $`y = {(y_{1},y_{2},\ldots,y_{N})} \in {({\mathbb{R}}^{d})}^{N}`$ that $`\mathcal{Y}_{0}^{x,y} = x`$ and

```math
\begin{array}{cl}
 & {{\mathcal{Y}_{n + 1}^{x,y} = {\mathcal{Y}_{n}^{x,y} + {\left( {\mathcal{R}_{a}\hspace{0pt}{(\rho_{{n + 1},y})}} \right)\hspace{0pt}{(\mathcal{Y}_{n}^{x,y})}}}}.}
\end{array}
```

Observe that item (i calculus ‣ Space-time error estimates for deep neural network approximations for differential equations")) in Proposition 2.6 calculus ‣ Space-time error estimates for deep neural network approximations for differential equations") and the fact that for all $`n \in {\{ 1,2,\ldots,N\}}`$, $`y = {(y_{1},y_{2},\ldots,y_{N})} \in {({\mathbb{R}}^{d})}^{N}`$ it holds that $`\rho_{n,y} = {{\mathbb{A}}_{n,y_{n}} \bullet \Phi}`$ prove that for all $`n \in {\{ 1,2,\ldots,N\}}`$, $`y \in {({\mathbb{R}}^{d})}^{N}`$ it holds that $`{\mathcal{D}\hspace{0pt}{(\rho_{n,y})}} = {\mathcal{D}\hspace{0pt}{(\Phi)}} = {(l_{0},l_{1},\ldots,l_{\mathcal{L}\hspace{0pt}{(\Phi)}})}`$. Corollary 2.31 calculus ‣ Space-time error estimates for deep neural network approximations for differential equations") (with $`a = a`$, $`d = d`$, $`{\mathfrak{i}} = {\mathfrak{i}}`$, $`L = {\mathcal{L}\hspace{0pt}{(\Phi)}}`$, $`{\mathfrak{L}} = 2`$, $`\ell_{0} = d`$, $`\ell_{1} = {\mathfrak{i}}`$, $`\ell_{2} = d`$, $`{\mathbb{I}} = {\mathbb{I}}`$, $`\psi = {\mathbb{I}}`$, $`{({{\mathbb{N}}_{0} \ni n\mapsto\phi_{n} \in \mathbf{N}})} = {({{\mathbb{N}}_{0} \ni n\mapsto\rho_{{n + 1},y} \in \mathbf{N}})}`$, $`{({{{\mathbb{N}}_{0} \times {\{ 0,1,\ldots,{\mathcal{L}\hspace{0pt}{(\Phi)}}\}}} \ni {(n,k)}\mapsto l_{n,k} \in {\mathbb{N}}})} = {({{{\mathbb{N}}_{0} \times {\{ 0,1,\ldots,{\mathcal{L}\hspace{0pt}{(\Phi)}}\}}} \ni {(n,k)}\mapsto l_{k} \in {\mathbb{N}}})}`$, $`{({{\mathbb{N}}_{0} \ni n\mapsto f_{n} \in {C\hspace{0pt}{({\mathbb{R}}^{d},{\mathbb{R}}^{d})}}})} = {({{\mathbb{N}}_{0} \ni n\mapsto{({{\mathbb{R}}^{d} \ni x\mapsto\mathcal{Y}_{n}^{x,y} \in {\mathbb{R}}^{d}})} \in {C\hspace{0pt}{({\mathbb{R}}^{d},{\mathbb{R}}^{d})}}})}`$ for $`y \in {({\mathbb{R}}^{d})}^{N}`$ in the notation of Corollary 2.31 calculus ‣ Space-time error estimates for deep neural network approximations for differential equations")) and the fact that for all $`x \in {\mathbb{R}}^{d}`$, $`y \in {({\mathbb{R}}^{d})}^{N}`$ it holds that $`{{({\mathcal{R}_{a}\hspace{0pt}{({\mathbb{I}})}})}\hspace{0pt}{(x)}} = x = \mathcal{Y}_{0}^{x,y} = Y_{0}^{x,y}`$ hence prove that there exist $`\Psi_{n,y} \in \mathbf{N}`$, $`{(n,y)} \in {{\{ 0,1,\ldots,N\}} \times {({\mathbb{R}}^{d})}^{N}}`$, which satisfy that

1.  (I)
    it holds for all $`n \in {\{ 0,1,\ldots,N\}}`$, $`y \in {({\mathbb{R}}^{d})}^{N}`$ that $`{\mathcal{R}_{a}\hspace{0pt}{(\Psi_{n,y})}} \in {C\hspace{0pt}{({\mathbb{R}}^{d},{\mathbb{R}}^{d})}}`$,
2.  (II)
    it holds for all $`n \in {\{ 0,1,\ldots,N\}}`$, $`y \in {({\mathbb{R}}^{d})}^{N}`$, $`x \in {\mathbb{R}}^{d}`$ that $`{{({\mathcal{R}_{a}\hspace{0pt}{(\Psi_{n,y})}})}\hspace{0pt}{(x)}} = \mathcal{Y}_{n}^{x,y} = Y_{n}^{x,y}`$,
3.  (III)
    it holds for all $`n \in {\{ 0,1,\ldots,N\}}`$, $`y \in {({\mathbb{R}}^{d})}^{N}`$ that

    ```math
    {{\mathcal{H}\hspace{0pt}{(\Psi_{n,y})}} = {{\mathcal{H}\hspace{0pt}{({\mathbb{I}})}} + {\sum\limits_{k = 0}^{n - 1}{\mathcal{H}\hspace{0pt}{(\rho_{{k + 1},y})}}}} = {1 + {n\hspace{0pt}\mathcal{H}\hspace{0pt}{(\Phi)}}}},
    ```

    and

4.  (IV)
    it holds for all $`n \in {\{ 0,1,\ldots,N\}}`$, $`y \in {({\mathbb{R}}^{d})}^{N}`$ that

    ```math
    {{\mathcal{P}\hspace{0pt}{(\Psi_{n,y})}} \leq {{\mathcal{P}\hspace{0pt}{({\mathbb{I}})}} + {\sum\limits_{k = 0}^{n - 1}\left\lbrack {{\frac{1}{2}\hspace{0pt}\mathcal{P}\hspace{0pt}{({\mathbb{I}})}} + {\mathcal{P}\hspace{0pt}{(\rho_{{k + 1},y})}}} \right\rbrack^{2}}} = {{\mathcal{P}\hspace{0pt}{({\mathbb{I}})}} + {n\hspace{0pt}\left\lbrack {{\frac{1}{2}\hspace{0pt}\mathcal{P}\hspace{0pt}{({\mathbb{I}})}} + {\mathcal{P}\hspace{0pt}{(\Phi)}}} \right\rbrack^{2}}}}.
    ```

Next we claim that for all $`n \in {\{ 0,1,\ldots,N\}}`$ it holds that

```math
{{{\forall x} \in {\mathbb{R}}^{d}}:\left\lbrack {\left( {{({\mathbb{R}}^{d})}^{N} \ni y\mapsto Y_{n}^{x,y} \in {\mathbb{R}}^{d}} \right) \in {C\hspace{0pt}\left( {({\mathbb{R}}^{d})}^{N},{\mathbb{R}}^{d} \right)}} \right\rbrack}.
```

We now prove (207 calculus ‣ Space-time error estimates for deep neural network approximations for differential equations")) by induction on $`n \in {\{ 0,1,\ldots,N\}}`$. Note that the fact that for all $`x \in {\mathbb{R}}^{d}`$, $`y \in {({\mathbb{R}}^{d})}^{N}`$ it holds that $`Y_{0}^{x,y} = x`$ proves (207 calculus ‣ Space-time error estimates for deep neural network approximations for differential equations")) in the base case $`n = 0`$. For the induction step observe that (197 calculus ‣ Space-time error estimates for deep neural network approximations for differential equations")) and the fact that $`{\mathcal{R}_{a}\hspace{0pt}{(\Phi)}} \in {C\hspace{0pt}{({\mathbb{R}}^{d},{\mathbb{R}}^{d})}}`$ ensure that for all $`n \in {\{ 0,1,\ldots,{N - 1}\}}`$ with

```math
{{\forall x} \in {\mathbb{R}}^{d}}:\left\lbrack {\left( {{({\mathbb{R}}^{d})}^{N} \ni y\mapsto Y_{n}^{x,y} \in {\mathbb{R}}^{d}} \right) \in {C\hspace{0pt}\left( {({\mathbb{R}}^{d})}^{N},{\mathbb{R}}^{d} \right)}} \right\rbrack
```

it holds that

```math
{{{\forall x} \in {\mathbb{R}}^{d}}:\left\lbrack {\left( {{({\mathbb{R}}^{d})}^{N} \ni y\mapsto Y_{n + 1}^{x,y} \in {\mathbb{R}}^{d}} \right) \in {C\hspace{0pt}\left( {({\mathbb{R}}^{d})}^{N},{\mathbb{R}}^{d} \right)}} \right\rbrack}.
```

Induction thus proves (207 calculus ‣ Space-time error estimates for deep neural network approximations for differential equations")). In addition, observe that (207 calculus ‣ Space-time error estimates for deep neural network approximations for differential equations")) and (II calculus ‣ Space-time error estimates for deep neural network approximations for differential equations")) imply that for all $`n \in {\{ 0,1,\ldots,N\}}`$, $`x \in {\mathbb{R}}^{d}`$ it holds that

```math
{\left( {{({\mathbb{R}}^{d})}^{N} \ni y\mapsto{{({\mathcal{R}_{a}\hspace{0pt}{(\Psi_{n,y})}})}\hspace{0pt}{(x)}} \in {\mathbb{R}}^{d}} \right) \in {C\hspace{0pt}\left( {({\mathbb{R}}^{d})}^{N},{\mathbb{R}}^{d} \right)}}.
```

Next let $`n \in {\{ 0,1,\ldots,N\}}`$, $`x \in {\mathbb{R}}^{d}`$, $`{y = {(y_{1},y_{2},\ldots,y_{N})}},{z = {(z_{1},z_{2},\ldots,z_{N})} \in {({\mathbb{R}}^{d})}^{N}}`$ satisfy for all $`k \in {{\mathbb{N}} \cap {\lbrack 0,n\rbrack}}`$ that $`y_{k} = z_{k}`$. We claim that for all $`m \in {{\mathbb{N}}_{0} \cap {\lbrack 0,n\rbrack}}`$ it holds that

```math
{Y_{m}^{x,y} = Y_{m}^{x,z}}.
```

We now prove (211 calculus ‣ Space-time error estimates for deep neural network approximations for differential equations")) by induction on $`m \in {{\mathbb{N}}_{0} \cap {\lbrack 0,n\rbrack}}`$. Note that the fact that $`Y_{0}^{x,y} = x = Y_{0}^{x,z}`$ implies (211 calculus ‣ Space-time error estimates for deep neural network approximations for differential equations")) in the base case $`m = 0`$. For the induction step observe that (197 calculus ‣ Space-time error estimates for deep neural network approximations for differential equations")) and the fact that for all $`k \in {{\mathbb{N}} \cap {\lbrack 0,n\rbrack}}`$ it holds that $`y_{k} = z_{k}`$ ensure that for all $`m \in {{\mathbb{N}}_{0} \cap {({- \infty},n)}}`$ with $`Y_{m}^{x,y} = Y_{m}^{x,z}`$ it holds that

```math
\begin{array}{cl}
Y_{m + 1}^{x,y} & {= {Y_{m}^{x,y} + {A_{m + 1}\hspace{0pt}\left( {{({\mathcal{R}_{a}\hspace{0pt}{(\Phi)}})}\hspace{0pt}{(Y_{m}^{x,y})}} \right)} + y_{m + 1}}} \\
 & {{= {Y_{m}^{x,z} + {A_{m + 1}\hspace{0pt}\left( {{({\mathcal{R}_{a}\hspace{0pt}{(\Phi)}})}\hspace{0pt}{(Y_{m}^{x,z})}} \right)} + z_{m + 1}} = Y_{m + 1}^{x,z}}.}
\end{array}
```

Induction thus proves (211 calculus ‣ Space-time error estimates for deep neural network approximations for differential equations")). Note that (211 calculus ‣ Space-time error estimates for deep neural network approximations for differential equations")) and (II calculus ‣ Space-time error estimates for deep neural network approximations for differential equations")) assure that for all $`n \in {\{ 0,1,\ldots,N\}}`$, $`m \in {{\mathbb{N}}_{0} \cap {\lbrack 0,n\rbrack}}`$, $`x \in {\mathbb{R}}^{d}`$, $`y = {(y_{1},y_{2},\ldots,y_{N})}`$, $`z = {(z_{1},z_{2},\ldots,z_{N})} \in {({\mathbb{R}}^{d})}^{N}`$ with $`{{\forall k} \in {{\mathbb{N}} \cap {\lbrack 0,n\rbrack}}}:{y_{k} = z_{k}}`$ it holds that

```math
{{{({\mathcal{R}_{a}\hspace{0pt}{(\Psi_{m,y})}})}\hspace{0pt}{(x)}} = {{({\mathcal{R}_{a}\hspace{0pt}{(\Psi_{m,z})}})}\hspace{0pt}{(x)}}}.
```

Combining this with (210 calculus ‣ Space-time error estimates for deep neural network approximations for differential equations")) and (I calculus ‣ Space-time error estimates for deep neural network approximations for differential equations"))–(IV calculus ‣ Space-time error estimates for deep neural network approximations for differential equations")) establishes items (i calculus ‣ Space-time error estimates for deep neural network approximations for differential equations"))–(vi calculus ‣ Space-time error estimates for deep neural network approximations for differential equations")). The proof of Proposition 2.32 calculus ‣ Space-time error estimates for deep neural network approximations for differential equations") is thus completed. ∎

## 3 ANN approximation results

This section establishes in Theorem 3.12 in Subsection 3.3 below the main result of this article. Some of the material presented in Subsection 3.1 and Subsection 3.2 are well-known concepts and results in the scientific literature. In particular, the material in Subsection 3.1.1 and Subsection 3.1.2 consists mainly of reformulations of concepts and results in Elbrächter et al. \[11, Appendix A.3 and Appendix A.4\]. Moreover, our proof of Proposition 3.5 in Subsection 3.2.1 below is inspired by Elbrächter et al. \[11, Section 6\] and Yarotsky \[37, Section 3.2\]. Furthermore, Lemma 3.8 and Lemma 3.9 are elementary and essentially well-known in the scientific literature. In addition, our proof of Lemma 3.11 is based on a well-known Gronwall argument.

### 3.1 ANN approximations for the square function

#### 3.1.1 Explicit approximations for the square function on $`\lbrack 0,1\rbrack`$

###### Lemma 3.1.

Let $`g_{n}:{{\mathbb{R}}\rightarrow{\lbrack 0,1\rbrack}}`$, $`n \in {\mathbb{N}}`$, be the functions which satisfy for all $`n \in {\mathbb{N}}`$, $`x \in {\mathbb{R}}`$ that

$`{g_{1}\hspace{0pt}{(x)}} = \begin{cases}
{2\hspace{0pt}x} & {:{x \in {\lbrack 0,\frac{1}{2})}}} \\
{2 - {2\hspace{0pt}x}} & {:{x \in {\lbrack\frac{1}{2},1\rbrack}}} \\
0 & {:{x \in {{\mathbb{R}}\backslash{\lbrack 0,1\rbrack}}}}
\end{cases}`$

and $`{g_{n + 1}\hspace{0pt}{(x)}} = {g_{1}\hspace{0pt}{({g_{n}\hspace{0pt}{(x)}})}}`$. Then

1.  (i)
    it holds for all $`n \in {\mathbb{N}}`$, $`k \in {\{ 0,1,\ldots,{2^{n - 1} - 1}\}}`$, $`x \in \left\lbrack \frac{k}{2^{n - 1}},\frac{k + 1}{2^{n - 1}} \right\rbrack`$ that

    ```math
    {g_{n}\hspace{0pt}{(x)}} = \begin{cases}
    {2^{n}\hspace{0pt}{({x - \frac{2\hspace{0pt}k}{2^{n}}})}} & {:{x \in \left\lbrack \frac{2\hspace{0pt}k}{2^{n}},\frac{{2\hspace{0pt}k} + 1}{2^{n}} \right\rbrack}} \\
    {2^{n}\hspace{0pt}{({\frac{{2\hspace{0pt}k} + 2}{2^{n}} - x})}} & {:{x \in \left\lbrack \frac{{2\hspace{0pt}k} + 1}{2^{n}},\frac{{2\hspace{0pt}k} + 2}{2^{n}} \right\rbrack}}
    \end{cases}
    ```

    and

2.  (ii)
    it holds for all $`n \in {\mathbb{N}}`$, $`x \in {{\mathbb{R}}\backslash{\lbrack 0,1\rbrack}}`$ that $`{g_{n}\hspace{0pt}{(x)}} = 0`$.

###### Proof of Lemma 3.1.

First, we claim that for all $`n \in {\mathbb{N}}`$ it holds that

```math
\begin{matrix}
\left( \forall k \in {\{ 0,1,\ldots,2^{n - 1} - 1\}},x \in \left\lbrack \frac{k}{2^{n - 1}},\frac{k + 1}{2^{n - 1}} \right\rbrack: \right. \\
{\left. g_{n}{(x)} = \begin{cases}
{2^{n}\hspace{0pt}{({x - \frac{2\hspace{0pt}k}{2^{n}}})}} & {:{x \in \left\lbrack \frac{2\hspace{0pt}k}{2^{n}},\frac{{2\hspace{0pt}k} + 1}{2^{n}} \right\rbrack}} \\
{2^{n}\hspace{0pt}{({\frac{{2\hspace{0pt}k} + 2}{2^{n}} - x})}} & {:{x \in \left\lbrack \frac{{2\hspace{0pt}k} + 1}{2^{n}},\frac{{2\hspace{0pt}k} + 2}{2^{n}} \right\rbrack}}
\end{cases} \right).}
\end{matrix}
```

We now prove (216) by induction on $`n \in {\mathbb{N}}`$. Note that (214) establishes (216) in the base case $`n = 1`$. For the induction step $`{\mathbb{N}} \ni n\rightarrow{n + 1} \in {{\mathbb{N}} \cap {\lbrack 2,\infty)}}`$ assume that there exists $`n \in {\mathbb{N}}`$ such that for all $`k \in {\{ 0,1,\ldots,{2^{n - 1} - 1}\}}`$, $`x \in \left\lbrack \frac{k}{2^{n - 1}},\frac{k + 1}{2^{n - 1}} \right\rbrack`$ it holds that

```math
{{g_{n}\hspace{0pt}{(x)}} = \begin{cases}
{2^{n}\hspace{0pt}{({x - \frac{2\hspace{0pt}k}{2^{n}}})}} & {:{x \in \left\lbrack \frac{2\hspace{0pt}k}{2^{n}},\frac{{2\hspace{0pt}k} + 1}{2^{n}} \right\rbrack}} \\
{2^{n}\hspace{0pt}{({\frac{{2\hspace{0pt}k} + 2}{2^{n}} - x})}} & {:{x \in \left\lbrack \frac{{2\hspace{0pt}k} + 1}{2^{n}},\frac{{2\hspace{0pt}k} + 2}{2^{n}} \right\rbrack}}
\end{cases}}.
```

Observe that (214) and (217) imply that for all $`l \in {\{ 0,1,\ldots,{2^{n - 1} - 1}\}}`$, $`x \in \left\lbrack \frac{2\hspace{0pt}l}{2^{n}},\frac{{2\hspace{0pt}l} + {({1/2})}}{2^{n}} \right\rbrack`$ it holds that

```math
\begin{aligned}
{g_{n + 1}\hspace{0pt}{(x)}} & {{= {g_{1}\hspace{0pt}{({g_{n}\hspace{0pt}{(x)}})}} = {g_{1}\hspace{0pt}{({2^{n}\hspace{0pt}{({x - \frac{2\hspace{0pt}l}{2^{n}}})}})}} = {2\hspace{0pt}\left\lbrack {2^{n}\hspace{0pt}{({x - \frac{2\hspace{0pt}l}{2^{n}}})}} \right\rbrack} = {2^{n + 1}\hspace{0pt}{({x - \frac{2\hspace{0pt}l}{2^{n}}})}}}.}
\end{aligned}
```

In addition, note that (214) and (217) ensure that for all $`l \in {\{ 0,1,\ldots,{2^{n - 1} - 1}\}}`$, $`x \in \left\lbrack \frac{{2\hspace{0pt}l} + {({1/2})}}{2^{n}},\frac{{2\hspace{0pt}l} + 1}{2^{n}} \right\rbrack`$ it holds that

```math
\begin{array}{cl}
{g_{n + 1}\hspace{0pt}{(x)}} & {= {g_{1}\hspace{0pt}{({g_{n}\hspace{0pt}{(x)}})}} = {g_{1}\hspace{0pt}{({2^{n}\hspace{0pt}{({x - \frac{2\hspace{0pt}l}{2^{n}}})}})}} = {2 - {2\hspace{0pt}\left\lbrack {2^{n}\hspace{0pt}{({x - \frac{2\hspace{0pt}l}{2^{n}}})}} \right\rbrack}}} \\
 & {{= {{2 - {2^{n + 1}\hspace{0pt}x}} + {4\hspace{0pt}l}} = {2^{n + 1}\hspace{0pt}{({\frac{{4\hspace{0pt}l} + 2}{2^{n + 1}} - x})}}}.}
\end{array}
```

Moreover, observe that (214) and (217) demonstrate that for all $`l \in {\{ 0,1,\ldots,{2^{n - 1} - 1}\}}`$, $`x \in \left\lbrack \frac{{2\hspace{0pt}l} + 1}{2^{n}},\frac{{2\hspace{0pt}l} + {({3/2})}}{2^{n}} \right\rbrack`$ it holds that

```math
\begin{array}{cl}
{g_{n + 1}\hspace{0pt}{(x)}} & {= {g_{1}\hspace{0pt}{({g_{n}\hspace{0pt}{(x)}})}} = {g_{1}\hspace{0pt}{({2^{n}\hspace{0pt}{({\frac{{2\hspace{0pt}l} + 2}{2^{n}} - x})}})}} = {2 - {2\hspace{0pt}\left\lbrack {2^{n}\hspace{0pt}{({\frac{{2\hspace{0pt}l} + 2}{2^{n}} - x})}} \right\rbrack}}} \\
 & {= {{2 - {2\hspace{0pt}{({{2\hspace{0pt}l} + 2})}}} + {2^{n + 1}\hspace{0pt}x}} = {{2^{n + 1}\hspace{0pt}x} - {4\hspace{0pt}l} - 2}} \\
 & {{= {2^{n + 1}\hspace{0pt}{({x - \frac{{4\hspace{0pt}l} + 2}{2^{n + 1}}})}}}.}
\end{array}
```

Next note that (214) and (217) prove that for all $`l \in {\{ 0,1,\ldots,{2^{n - 1} - 1}\}}`$, $`x \in \left\lbrack \frac{{2\hspace{0pt}l} + {({3/2})}}{2^{n}},\frac{{2\hspace{0pt}l} + 2}{2^{n}} \right\rbrack`$ it holds that

```math
\begin{aligned}
{g_{n + 1}\hspace{0pt}{(x)}} & {{= {g_{1}\hspace{0pt}{({g_{n}\hspace{0pt}{(x)}})}} = {g_{1}\hspace{0pt}{({2^{n}\hspace{0pt}{({\frac{{2\hspace{0pt}l} + 2}{2^{n}} - x})}})}} = {2\hspace{0pt}\left\lbrack {2^{n}\hspace{0pt}{({\frac{{2\hspace{0pt}l} + 2}{2^{n}} - x})}} \right\rbrack} = {2^{n + 1}\hspace{0pt}{({\frac{{2\hspace{0pt}l} + 2}{2^{n}} - x})}}}.}
\end{aligned}
```

Moreover, observe that for all $`k \in {{\{ 0,2,4,6,\ldots\}} \cap {\lbrack 0,{2^{n} - 2}\rbrack}}`$ it holds that

```math
{{\left\lbrack \frac{2\hspace{0pt}k}{2^{n + 1}},\frac{{2\hspace{0pt}k} + 1}{2^{n + 1}} \right\rbrack = \left\lbrack \frac{2\hspace{0pt}{({k/2})}}{2^{n}},\frac{{2\hspace{0pt}{({k/2})}} + {({1/2})}}{2^{n}} \right\rbrack},{\left\lbrack \frac{{2\hspace{0pt}k} + 1}{2^{n + 1}},\frac{{2\hspace{0pt}k} + 2}{2^{n + 1}} \right\rbrack = \left\lbrack \frac{{2\hspace{0pt}{({k/2})}} + {({1/2})}}{2^{n}},\frac{{2\hspace{0pt}{({k/2})}} + 1}{2^{n}} \right\rbrack}},
```

and $`{k/2} \in {\{ 0,1,\ldots,{2^{n - 1} - 1}\}}`$. This, (218), and (219) demonstrate that for all $`k \in {{\{ 0,2,4,6,\ldots\}} \cap {\lbrack 0,{2^{n} - 2}\rbrack}}`$, $`x \in \left\lbrack \frac{k}{2^{n}},\frac{k + 1}{2^{n}} \right\rbrack`$ it holds that

```math
\begin{array}{cl}
{g_{n + 1}\hspace{0pt}{(x)}} & {= \begin{cases}
{2^{n + 1}\hspace{0pt}{({x - \frac{2\hspace{0pt}{({k/2})}}{2^{n}}})}} & {:{x \in \left\lbrack \frac{2\hspace{0pt}{({k/2})}}{2^{n}},\frac{{2\hspace{0pt}{({k/2})}} + {({1/2})}}{2^{n}} \right\rbrack}} \\
{2^{n + 1}\hspace{0pt}{({\frac{{4\hspace{0pt}{({k/2})}} + 2}{2^{n + 1}} - x})}} & {:{x \in \left\lbrack \frac{{2\hspace{0pt}{({k/2})}} + {({1/2})}}{2^{n}},\frac{{2\hspace{0pt}{({k/2})}} + 1}{2^{n}} \right\rbrack}}
\end{cases}} \\
 & {{= \begin{cases}
{2^{n + 1}\hspace{0pt}{({x - \frac{2\hspace{0pt}k}{2^{n + 1}}})}} & {:{x \in \left\lbrack \frac{2\hspace{0pt}k}{2^{n + 1}},\frac{{2\hspace{0pt}k} + 1}{2^{n + 1}} \right\rbrack}} \\
{2^{n + 1}\hspace{0pt}{({\frac{{2\hspace{0pt}k} + 2}{2^{n + 1}} - x})}} & {:{x \in \left\lbrack \frac{{2\hspace{0pt}k} + 1}{2^{n + 1}},\frac{{2\hspace{0pt}k} + 2}{2^{n + 1}} \right\rbrack}}
\end{cases}}.}
\end{array}
```

In addition, observe that for all $`k \in {{\{ 1,3,5,7,\ldots\}} \cap {\lbrack 1,{2^{n} - 1}\rbrack}}`$ it holds that

```math
\begin{array}{r}
{{\left\lbrack \frac{2\hspace{0pt}k}{2^{n + 1}},\frac{{2\hspace{0pt}k} + 1}{2^{n + 1}} \right\rbrack = \left\lbrack \frac{{2\hspace{0pt}{({\left( {k - 1} \right)/2})}} + 1}{2^{n}},\frac{{2\hspace{0pt}{({\left( {k - 1} \right)/2})}} + {({3/2})}}{2^{n}} \right\rbrack},} \\
{{\left\lbrack \frac{{2\hspace{0pt}k} + 1}{2^{n + 1}},\frac{{2\hspace{0pt}k} + 2}{2^{n + 1}} \right\rbrack = \left\lbrack \frac{{2\hspace{0pt}{({\left( {k - 1} \right)/2})}} + {({3/2})}}{2^{n}},\frac{{2\hspace{0pt}{({\left( {k - 1} \right)/2})}} + 2}{2^{n}} \right\rbrack},}
\end{array}
```

and $`{\left( {k - 1} \right)/2} \in {\{ 0,1,\ldots,{2^{n - 1} - 1}\}}`$. This, (220), and (221) demonstrate that for all $`k \in {{\{ 1,3,5,7,\ldots\}} \cap {\lbrack 1,{2^{n} - 1}\rbrack}}`$, $`x \in \left\lbrack \frac{k}{2^{n}},\frac{k + 1}{2^{n}} \right\rbrack`$ it holds that

```math
\begin{array}{cl}
{g_{n + 1}\hspace{0pt}{(x)}} & {= \begin{cases}
{2^{n + 1}\hspace{0pt}{({x - \frac{{4\hspace{0pt}{({\left( {k - 1} \right)/2})}} + 2}{2^{n + 1}}})}} & {:{x \in \left\lbrack \frac{{2\hspace{0pt}{({\left( {k - 1} \right)/2})}} + 1}{2^{n}},\frac{{2\hspace{0pt}{({\left( {k - 1} \right)/2})}} + {({3/2})}}{2^{n}} \right\rbrack}} \\
{2^{n + 1}\hspace{0pt}{({\frac{{2\hspace{0pt}{({\left( {k - 1} \right)/2})}} + 2}{2^{n}} - x})}} & {:{x \in \left\lbrack \frac{{2\hspace{0pt}{({\left( {k - 1} \right)/2})}} + {({3/2})}}{2^{n}},\frac{{2\hspace{0pt}{({\left( {k - 1} \right)/2})}} + 2}{2^{n}} \right\rbrack}}
\end{cases}} \\
 & {{= \begin{cases}
{2^{n + 1}\hspace{0pt}{({x - \frac{2\hspace{0pt}k}{2^{n + 1}}})}} & {:{x \in \left\lbrack \frac{2\hspace{0pt}k}{2^{n + 1}},\frac{{2\hspace{0pt}k} + 1}{2^{n + 1}} \right\rbrack}} \\
{2^{n + 1}\hspace{0pt}{({\frac{{2\hspace{0pt}k} + 2}{2^{n + 1}} - x})}} & {:{x \in \left\lbrack \frac{{2\hspace{0pt}k} + 1}{2^{n + 1}},\frac{{2\hspace{0pt}k} + 2}{2^{n + 1}} \right\rbrack}}
\end{cases}}.}
\end{array}
```

Combining this with (223) ensures that for all $`k \in {\{ 0,1,\ldots,{2^{n} - 1}\}}`$, $`x \in \left\lbrack \frac{k}{2^{n}},\frac{k + 1}{2^{n}} \right\rbrack`$ it holds that

```math
\begin{array}{r}
{{{g_{n + 1}\hspace{0pt}{(x)}} = \begin{cases}
{2^{n + 1}\hspace{0pt}{({x - \frac{2\hspace{0pt}k}{2^{n + 1}}})}} & {:{x \in \left\lbrack \frac{2\hspace{0pt}k}{2^{n + 1}},\frac{{2\hspace{0pt}k} + 1}{2^{n + 1}} \right\rbrack}} \\
{2^{n + 1}\hspace{0pt}{({\frac{{2\hspace{0pt}k} + 2}{2^{n + 1}} - x})}} & {:{x \in \left\lbrack \frac{{2\hspace{0pt}k} + 1}{2^{n + 1}},\frac{{2\hspace{0pt}k} + 2}{2^{n + 1}} \right\rbrack}}
\end{cases}}.}
\end{array}
```

Induction thus proves (216). Observe that (216) establishes item (i). Next we claim that for all $`n \in {\mathbb{N}}`$ it holds that

```math
{{{\forall x} \in {{\mathbb{R}}\backslash{\lbrack 0,1\rbrack}}}:{{g_{n}\hspace{0pt}{(x)}} = 0}}.
```

We now prove (227) by induction on $`n \in {\mathbb{N}}`$. Note that (214) establishes (227) in the base case $`n = 1`$. For the induction step observe that (214) ensures that for all $`n \in {\mathbb{N}}`$ with $`({{{\forall x} \in {{\mathbb{R}}\backslash{\lbrack 0,1\rbrack}}}:{{g_{n}\hspace{0pt}{(x)}} = 0}})`$ it holds that

```math
\left( {{{\forall x} \in {{\mathbb{R}}\backslash{\lbrack 0,1\rbrack}}}:{{g_{n + 1}\hspace{0pt}{(x)}} = {g_{1}\hspace{0pt}{({g_{n}\hspace{0pt}{(x)}})}} = {g_{1}\hspace{0pt}{(0)}} = 0}} \right).
```

Induction thus proves (227). Note that (227) establishes item (ii). The proof of Lemma 3.1 is thus completed. ∎

###### Lemma 3.2.

Let $`g_{n}:{{\lbrack 0,1\rbrack}\rightarrow{\lbrack 0,1\rbrack}}`$, $`n \in {\mathbb{N}}`$, be the functions which satisfy for all $`n \in {\mathbb{N}}`$, $`x \in {\lbrack 0,1\rbrack}`$ that

```math
{g_{1}\hspace{0pt}{(x)}} = \begin{cases}
{2\hspace{0pt}x} & {:{x \in {\lbrack 0,\frac{1}{2})}}} \\
{2 - {2\hspace{0pt}x}} & {:{x \in {\lbrack\frac{1}{2},1\rbrack}}}
\end{cases}
```

and $`{g_{n + 1}\hspace{0pt}{(x)}} = {g_{1}\hspace{0pt}{({g_{n}\hspace{0pt}{(x)}})}}`$, and let $`f_{n}:{{\lbrack 0,1\rbrack}\rightarrow{\lbrack 0,1\rbrack}}`$, $`n \in {\mathbb{N}}_{0}`$, be the functions which satisfy for all $`n \in {\mathbb{N}}_{0}`$, $`k \in {\{ 0,1,\ldots,{2^{n} - 1}\}}`$, $`x \in \left\lbrack \frac{k}{2^{n}},\frac{k + 1}{2^{n}} \right)`$ that $`{f_{n}\hspace{0pt}{(1)}} = 1`$ and

```math
{{f_{n}\hspace{0pt}{(x)}} = {{\left\lbrack \frac{{2\hspace{0pt}k} + 1}{2^{n}} \right\rbrack\hspace{0pt}x} - \frac{({k^{2} + k})}{2^{2\hspace{0pt}n}}}}.
```

Then it holds for all $`n \in {\mathbb{N}}_{0}`$, $`x \in {\lbrack 0,1\rbrack}`$ that

```math
{{{f_{n}\hspace{0pt}{(x)}} = {{x - \left\lbrack {\sum\limits_{m = 1}^{n}\left( {2^{- {2\hspace{0pt}m}}\hspace{0pt}g_{m}\hspace{0pt}{(x)}} \right)} \right\rbrack}\qquad\text{and}}}\qquad{\left| {x^{2} - {f_{n}\hspace{0pt}{(x)}}} \right| \leq 2^{{- {2\hspace{0pt}n}} - 2}}}.
```

###### Proof of Lemma 3.2.

Note that (230) proves that for all $`n \in {\mathbb{N}}_{0}`$, $`l \in {\{ 0,1,\ldots,{2^{n} - 1}\}}`$ it holds that

```math
\begin{array}{r}
{{{f_{n}\hspace{0pt}{(\frac{l}{2^{n}})}} = {{\left\lbrack \frac{{2\hspace{0pt}l} + 1}{2^{n}} \right\rbrack\hspace{0pt}\frac{l}{2^{n}}} - \frac{({l^{2} + l})}{2^{2\hspace{0pt}n}}} = \frac{{{({{2\hspace{0pt}l} + 1})}\hspace{0pt}l} - {({l^{2} + l})}}{2^{2\hspace{0pt}n}} = \frac{l^{2}}{2^{2\hspace{0pt}n}} = \left\lbrack \frac{l}{2^{n}} \right\rbrack^{2}}.}
\end{array}
```

The hypothesis that for all $`n \in {\mathbb{N}}_{0}`$ it holds that $`{f_{n}\hspace{0pt}{(1)}} = 1`$ hence ensures that for all $`n \in {\mathbb{N}}_{0}`$, $`l \in {\{ 0,1,\ldots,2^{n}\}}`$ it holds that

```math
\begin{array}{r}
{{{f_{n}\hspace{0pt}{(\frac{l}{2^{n}})}} = \left\lbrack \frac{l}{2^{n}} \right\rbrack^{2}}.}
\end{array}
```

This and Lemma 3.1 demonstrate that for all $`n \in {\mathbb{N}}`$, $`k \in {\{ 0,1,\ldots,2^{n - 1}\}}`$ it holds that

```math
\begin{array}{cl}
{{f_{n - 1}\hspace{0pt}{(\frac{2\hspace{0pt}k}{2^{n}})}} - {f_{n}\hspace{0pt}{(\frac{2\hspace{0pt}k}{2^{n}})}}} & {= {{f_{n - 1}\hspace{0pt}{(\frac{k}{2^{n - 1}})}} - {f_{n}\hspace{0pt}{(\frac{2\hspace{0pt}k}{2^{n}})}}} = {\left\lbrack \frac{k}{2^{n - 1}} \right\rbrack^{2} - \left\lbrack \frac{2\hspace{0pt}k}{2^{n}} \right\rbrack^{2}}} \\
 & {{= 0 = {2^{- {2\hspace{0pt}n}}\hspace{0pt}g_{n}\hspace{0pt}{(\frac{2\hspace{0pt}k}{2^{n}})}}}.}
\end{array}
```

In addition, note that (230) and (233) imply that for all $`n \in {\mathbb{N}}`$, $`k \in {\{ 0,1,\ldots,{2^{n - 1} - 1}\}}`$ it holds that

```math
\begin{array}{cl}
{{f_{n - 1}\hspace{0pt}{(\frac{{2\hspace{0pt}k} + 1}{2^{n}})}} = {f_{n - 1}\hspace{0pt}\left( \frac{k + {({1/2})}}{2^{n - 1}} \right)}} & {= {{\left\lbrack \frac{{2\hspace{0pt}k} + 1}{2^{n - 1}} \right\rbrack\hspace{0pt}\left\lbrack \frac{{2\hspace{0pt}k} + 1}{2^{n}} \right\rbrack} - \frac{({k^{2} + k})}{2^{2\hspace{0pt}{({n - 1})}}}} = {\frac{({{4\hspace{0pt}k^{2}} + {4\hspace{0pt}k} + 1})}{2^{{2\hspace{0pt}n} - 1}} - \frac{({{2\hspace{0pt}k^{2}} + {2\hspace{0pt}k}})}{2^{{2\hspace{0pt}n} - 1}}}} \\
 & {= \frac{{2\hspace{0pt}k^{2}} + {2\hspace{0pt}k} + 1}{2^{{2\hspace{0pt}n} - 1}} = \frac{{4\hspace{0pt}k^{2}} + {4\hspace{0pt}k} + 2}{2^{2\hspace{0pt}n}}}
\end{array}
```

and

```math
\begin{aligned}
{f_{n}\hspace{0pt}{(\frac{{2\hspace{0pt}k} + 1}{2^{n}})}} & {{= \left\lbrack \frac{{2\hspace{0pt}k} + 1}{2^{n}} \right\rbrack^{2} = \frac{{4\hspace{0pt}k^{2}} + {4\hspace{0pt}k} + 1}{2^{2\hspace{0pt}n}}}.}
\end{aligned}
```

Lemma 3.1 hence assures that for all $`n \in {\mathbb{N}}`$, $`k \in {\{ 0,1,\ldots,{2^{n - 1} - 1}\}}`$ it holds that

```math
{{{f_{n - 1}\hspace{0pt}{(\frac{{2\hspace{0pt}k} + 1}{2^{n}})}} - {f_{n}\hspace{0pt}{(\frac{{2\hspace{0pt}k} + 1}{2^{n}})}}} = {\frac{({{4\hspace{0pt}k^{2}} + {4\hspace{0pt}k} + 2})}{2^{2\hspace{0pt}n}} - \frac{({{4\hspace{0pt}k^{2}} + {4\hspace{0pt}k} + 1})}{2^{2\hspace{0pt}n}}} = 2^{- {2\hspace{0pt}n}} = {2^{- {2\hspace{0pt}n}}\hspace{0pt}g_{n}\hspace{0pt}{(\frac{{2\hspace{0pt}k} + 1}{2^{n}})}}}.
```

Combining this with (234) shows that for all $`n \in {\mathbb{N}}`$, $`l \in {\{ 0,1,\ldots,2^{n}\}}`$ it holds that

```math
{{{f_{n - 1}\hspace{0pt}{(\frac{l}{2^{n}})}} - {f_{n}\hspace{0pt}{(\frac{l}{2^{n}})}}} = {2^{- {2\hspace{0pt}n}}\hspace{0pt}g_{n}\hspace{0pt}{(\frac{l}{2^{n}})}}}.
```

Furthermore, observe that (233) demonstrates that for all $`n \in {\mathbb{N}}_{0}`$, $`l \in {\{ 0,1,\ldots,{2^{n} - 1}\}}`$ it holds that

```math
\begin{array}{r}
{{{{\left\lbrack \frac{{2\hspace{0pt}l} + 1}{2^{n}} \right\rbrack\hspace{0pt}\left\lbrack \frac{l + 1}{2^{n}} \right\rbrack} - \frac{({l^{2} + l})}{2^{2\hspace{0pt}n}}} = \frac{{{({{2\hspace{0pt}l} + 1})}\hspace{0pt}{({l + 1})}} - {l\hspace{0pt}{({l + 1})}}}{2^{2\hspace{0pt}n}} = \frac{{({l + 1})}^{2}}{2^{2\hspace{0pt}n}} = \left\lbrack \frac{l + 1}{2^{n}} \right\rbrack^{2} = {f_{n}\hspace{0pt}{(\frac{l + 1}{2^{n}})}}}.}
\end{array}
```

Combining this with (230) implies that for all $`n \in {\mathbb{N}}_{0}`$ it holds that $`f_{n} \in {C\hspace{0pt}{({\lbrack 0,1\rbrack},{\mathbb{R}})}}`$ and

```math
{{{{\forall l} \in {\{ 0,1,\ldots,{2^{n} - 1}\}}},{x \in \left\lbrack \frac{l}{2^{n}},\frac{l + 1}{2^{n}} \right\rbrack}}:{{f_{n}\hspace{0pt}{(x)}} = {{\left\lbrack \frac{{2\hspace{0pt}l} + 1}{2^{n}} \right\rbrack\hspace{0pt}x} - \frac{({l^{2} + l})}{2^{2\hspace{0pt}n}}}}}.
```

The fact that for all $`n \in {\mathbb{N}}`$, $`k \in {\{ 0,1,\ldots,{2^{n - 1} - 1}\}}`$ it holds that $`\left\lbrack \frac{k}{2^{n - 1}},\frac{k + 1}{2^{n - 1}} \right\rbrack = {\left\lbrack \frac{2\hspace{0pt}k}{2^{n}},\frac{{2\hspace{0pt}k} + 1}{2^{n}} \right\rbrack \cup \left\lbrack \frac{{2\hspace{0pt}k} + 1}{2^{n}},\frac{{2\hspace{0pt}k} + 2}{2^{n}} \right\rbrack}`$ hence ensures that there exist $`{(a_{n,k},b_{n,k},c_{n,k})} \in {\mathbb{R}}^{3}`$, $`k \in {\{ 0,1,\ldots,{2^{n - 1} - 1}\}}`$, $`n \in {\mathbb{N}}`$, such that for all $`n \in {\mathbb{N}}`$, $`k \in {\{ 0,1,\ldots,{2^{n - 1} - 1}\}}`$, $`x \in \left\lbrack \frac{k}{2^{n - 1}},\frac{k + 1}{2^{n - 1}} \right\rbrack`$ it holds that

```math
{{{f_{n - 1}\hspace{0pt}{(x)}} - {f_{n}\hspace{0pt}{(x)}}} = \begin{cases}
{{a_{n,k}\hspace{0pt}\left( {x - \frac{({{2\hspace{0pt}k} + 1})}{2^{n}}} \right)} + b_{n,k}} & {:{x \in \left\lbrack \frac{2\hspace{0pt}k}{2^{n}},\frac{{2\hspace{0pt}k} + 1}{2^{n}} \right\rbrack}} \\
{{c_{n,k}\hspace{0pt}\left( {x - \frac{({{2\hspace{0pt}k} + 1})}{2^{n}}} \right)} + b_{n,k}} & {:{x \in \left\lbrack \frac{{2\hspace{0pt}k} + 1}{2^{n}},\frac{{2\hspace{0pt}k} + 2}{2^{n}} \right\rbrack}}
\end{cases}}.
```

Lemma 3.1 and (238) therefore prove that for all $`n \in {\mathbb{N}}`$, $`k \in {\{ 0,1,\ldots,{2^{n - 1} - 1}\}}`$, $`x \in \left\lbrack \frac{k}{2^{n - 1}},\frac{k + 1}{2^{n - 1}} \right\rbrack`$ it holds that

```math
{{{f_{n - 1}\hspace{0pt}{(x)}} - {f_{n}\hspace{0pt}{(x)}}} = {2^{- {2\hspace{0pt}n}}\hspace{0pt}g_{n}\hspace{0pt}{(x)}}}.
```

Hence, we obtain that for all $`n \in {\mathbb{N}}`$, $`x \in {\lbrack 0,1\rbrack}`$ it holds that

```math
{{{f_{n - 1}\hspace{0pt}{(x)}} - {f_{n}\hspace{0pt}{(x)}}} = {2^{- {2\hspace{0pt}n}}\hspace{0pt}g_{n}\hspace{0pt}{(x)}}}.
```

Next note that (230) ensures that for all $`x \in {\lbrack 0,1\rbrack}`$ it holds that $`{f_{0}\hspace{0pt}{(x)}} = x`$. Combining this with (243) implies that for all $`m \in {\mathbb{N}}_{0}`$, $`x \in {\lbrack 0,1\rbrack}`$ it holds that

```math
\begin{array}{cl}
{f_{m}\hspace{0pt}{(x)}} & {= {{f_{0}\hspace{0pt}{(x)}} + \left\lbrack {\sum\limits_{n = 1}^{m}{({{f_{n}\hspace{0pt}{(x)}} - {f_{n - 1}\hspace{0pt}{(x)}}})}} \right\rbrack}} \\
 & {{= {{f_{0}\hspace{0pt}{(x)}} - \left\lbrack {\sum\limits_{n = 1}^{m}{({{f_{n - 1}\hspace{0pt}{(x)}} - {f_{n}\hspace{0pt}{(x)}}})}} \right\rbrack} = {x - \left\lbrack {\sum\limits_{n = 1}^{m}{2^{- {2\hspace{0pt}n}}\hspace{0pt}g_{n}\hspace{0pt}{(x)}}} \right\rbrack}}.}
\end{array}
```

Moreover, observe that (240) demonstrates that for all $`m \in {\mathbb{N}}_{0}`$, $`l \in {\{ 0,1,\ldots,{2^{m} - 1}\}}`$, $`x \in \left\lbrack \frac{l}{2^{m}},\frac{l + 1}{2^{m}} \right\rbrack`$ it holds that

```math
\begin{array}{cl}
{{f_{m}\hspace{0pt}{(x)}} - x^{2}} & {= {{\left\lbrack \frac{{2\hspace{0pt}l} + 1}{2^{m}} \right\rbrack\hspace{0pt}x} - \frac{({l^{2} + l})}{2^{2\hspace{0pt}m}} - x^{2}} = {{{\left\lbrack \frac{l + 1}{2^{m}} \right\rbrack\hspace{0pt}x} + {\left\lbrack \frac{l}{2^{m}} \right\rbrack\hspace{0pt}x}} - {\left\lbrack \frac{l + 1}{2^{m}} \right\rbrack\hspace{0pt}\left\lbrack \frac{l}{2^{m}} \right\rbrack} - x^{2}}} \\
 & {{= {\left( {x - \frac{l}{2^{m}}} \right)\hspace{0pt}\left( {\frac{l + 1}{2^{m}} - x} \right)} \geq 0}.}
\end{array}
```

The fact that for all $`a \in {\mathbb{R}}`$, $`b \in {(a,\infty)}`$, $`r \in {\lbrack a,b\rbrack}`$ it holds that $`{{({r - a})}\hspace{0pt}{({b - r})}} \leq {\frac{1}{4}\hspace{0pt}{({b - a})}^{2}}`$ hence proves that for all $`m \in {\mathbb{N}}_{0}`$, $`l \in {\{ 0,1,\ldots,{2^{m} - 1}\}}`$, $`x \in \left\lbrack \frac{l}{2^{m}},\frac{l + 1}{2^{m}} \right\rbrack`$ it holds that

```math
\begin{array}{cl}
\left| {{f_{m}\hspace{0pt}{(x)}} - x^{2}} \right| & {= {{f_{m}\hspace{0pt}{(x)}} - x^{2}} = {\left( {x - \frac{l}{2^{m}}} \right)\hspace{0pt}\left( {\frac{l + 1}{2^{m}} - x} \right)}} \\
 & {{\leq {\frac{1}{4}\hspace{0pt}\left( {\frac{l + 1}{2^{m}} - \frac{l}{2^{m}}} \right)^{2}} = {\frac{1}{4}\hspace{0pt}\left( \frac{1}{2^{m}} \right)^{2}} = {\frac{1}{2^{2}}\hspace{0pt}\left( \frac{1}{2^{2\hspace{0pt}m}} \right)} = \frac{1}{2^{{2\hspace{0pt}m} + 2}} = 2^{{- {2\hspace{0pt}m}} - 2}}.}
\end{array}
```

Therefore, we obtain that for all $`m \in {\mathbb{N}}_{0}`$, $`x \in {\lbrack 0,1\rbrack}`$ it holds that

```math
{\left| {{f_{m}\hspace{0pt}{(x)}} - x^{2}} \right| \leq 2^{{- {2\hspace{0pt}m}} - 2}}.
```

Combining this with (244) establishes (231). The proof of Lemma 3.2 is thus completed. ∎

#### 3.1.2 ANN approximations for the square function on $`\lbrack 0,1\rbrack`$

###### Proposition 3.3.

Let $`\varepsilon \in {(0,1\rbrack}`$, $`a \in {C\hspace{0pt}{({\mathbb{R}},{\mathbb{R}})}}`$ satisfy for all $`x \in {\mathbb{R}}`$ that $`{a\hspace{0pt}{(x)}} = {\max{\{ x,0\}}}`$. Then there exists $`\Phi \in \mathbf{N}`$ such that

1.  (i)
    it holds that $`{\mathcal{R}_{a}\hspace{0pt}{(\Phi)}} \in {C\hspace{0pt}{({\mathbb{R}},{\mathbb{R}})}}`$,
2.  (ii)
    it holds for all $`x \in {{\mathbb{R}}\backslash{\lbrack 0,1\rbrack}}`$ that $`{{({\mathcal{R}_{a}\hspace{0pt}{(\Phi)}})}\hspace{0pt}{(x)}} = {a\hspace{0pt}{(x)}}`$,
3.  (iii)
    it holds for all $`x \in {\lbrack 0,1\rbrack}`$ that $`\left| {x^{2} - {{({\mathcal{R}_{a}\hspace{0pt}{(\Phi)}})}\hspace{0pt}{(x)}}} \right| \leq \varepsilon`$,
4.  (iv)
    it holds that $`{\mathcal{P}\hspace{0pt}{(\Phi)}} \leq {\max{\{{{10\hspace{0pt}{\log_{2}{(\varepsilon^{- 1})}}} - 7},13\}}}`$, and
5.  (v)
    it holds that $`{\mathcal{L}\hspace{0pt}{(\Phi)}} \leq {\max{\{{{\frac{1}{2}\hspace{0pt}{\log_{2}{(\varepsilon^{- 1})}}} + 1},2\}}}`$

(cf. Definition 2.1). ‣ 2.1 Artificial neural networks (ANNs) and their realizations ‣ 2 Artificial neural network (ANN) calculus ‣ Space-time error estimates for deep neural network approximations for differential equations") and Definition 2.3. ‣ 2.1 Artificial neural networks (ANNs) and their realizations ‣ 2 Artificial neural network (ANN) calculus ‣ Space-time error estimates for deep neural network approximations for differential equations")).

###### Proof of Proposition 3.3.

Throughout this proof let $`M \in {\mathbb{N}}`$ satisfy that

```math
{M = {\min\left( {{\mathbb{N}} \cap {\lbrack 2,\infty)} \cap \left\lbrack {\frac{1}{2}\hspace{0pt}{\log_{2}{(\varepsilon^{- 1})}}},\infty \right)} \right)}},
```

let $`g_{n}:{{\mathbb{R}}\rightarrow{\lbrack 0,1\rbrack}}`$, $`n \in {\mathbb{N}}`$, be the functions which satisfy for all $`n \in {\mathbb{N}}`$, $`x \in {\mathbb{R}}`$ that

```math
{g_{1}\hspace{0pt}{(x)}} = \begin{cases}
{2\hspace{0pt}x} & {:{x \in {\lbrack 0,\frac{1}{2})}}} \\
{2 - {2\hspace{0pt}x}} & {:{x \in {\lbrack\frac{1}{2},1\rbrack}}} \\
0 & {:{x \in {{\mathbb{R}}\backslash{\lbrack 0,1\rbrack}}}}
\end{cases}
```

and $`{g_{n + 1}\hspace{0pt}{(x)}} = {g_{1}\hspace{0pt}{({g_{n}\hspace{0pt}{(x)}})}}`$, let $`f_{n}:{{\lbrack 0,1\rbrack}\rightarrow{\lbrack 0,1\rbrack}}`$, $`n \in {\mathbb{N}}_{0}`$, be the functions which satisfy for all $`n \in {\mathbb{N}}_{0}`$, $`k \in {\{ 0,1,\ldots,{2^{n} - 1}\}}`$, $`x \in \left\lbrack \frac{k}{2^{n}},\frac{k + 1}{2^{n}} \right)`$ that $`{f_{n}\hspace{0pt}{(1)}} = 1`$ and

```math
{{f_{n}\hspace{0pt}{(x)}} = {{\left\lbrack \frac{{2\hspace{0pt}k} + 1}{2^{n}} \right\rbrack\hspace{0pt}x} - \frac{({k^{2} + k})}{2^{2\hspace{0pt}n}}}},
```

let $`{(A_{k},b_{k})} \in {{\mathbb{R}}^{4 \times 4} \times {\mathbb{R}}^{4}}`$, $`k \in {{\mathbb{N}} \cap {\lbrack 2,\infty)}}`$, satisfy for all $`k \in {{\mathbb{N}} \cap {\lbrack 2,\infty)}}`$ that

```math
{{A_{k} = {\begin{pmatrix}
2 & {- 4} & 2 & 0 \\
2 & {- 4} & 2 & 0 \\
2 & {- 4} & 2 & 0 \\
{({- 2})}^{3 - {2\hspace{0pt}k}} & 2^{4 - {2\hspace{0pt}k}} & {({- 2})}^{3 - {2\hspace{0pt}k}} & 1
\end{pmatrix}\quad{and}}}\quad{b_{k} = \begin{pmatrix}
0 \\
{- \frac{1}{2}} \\
{- 1} \\
0
\end{pmatrix}}},
```

let $`{\mathbb{A}}_{k} \in {{\mathbb{R}}^{1 \times 4} \times {\mathbb{R}}}`$, $`k \in {{\mathbb{N}} \cap {\lbrack 2,\infty)}}`$, satisfy for all $`k \in {{\mathbb{N}} \cap {\lbrack 2,\infty)}}`$ that

```math
{{\mathbb{A}}_{k} = \left( \begin{pmatrix}
{({- 2})}^{3 - {2\hspace{0pt}k}} & 2^{4 - {2\hspace{0pt}k}} & {({- 2})}^{3 - {2\hspace{0pt}k}} & 1
\end{pmatrix},0 \right)},
```

let $`\phi_{k} \in \mathbf{N}`$, $`k \in {{\mathbb{N}} \cap {\lbrack 2,\infty)}}`$, satisfy that

```math
\phi_{2} = \left( \left( \begin{pmatrix}
1 \\
1 \\
1 \\
1
\end{pmatrix},\begin{pmatrix}
0 \\
{- \frac{1}{2}} \\
{- 1} \\
0
\end{pmatrix} \right),{\mathbb{A}}_{2} \right)
```

and

```math
{{{\forall k} \in {{\mathbb{N}} \cap {\lbrack 3,\infty)}}}:{\phi_{k} = \left( \left( \begin{pmatrix}
1 \\
1 \\
1 \\
1
\end{pmatrix},\begin{pmatrix}
0 \\
{- \frac{1}{2}} \\
{- 1} \\
0
```

and let $`{r_{k} = {(r_{k,1},r_{k,2},r_{k,3},r_{k,4})}}:{{\mathbb{R}}\rightarrow{\mathbb{R}}^{4}}`$, $`k \in {\mathbb{N}}`$, be the functions which satisfy for all $`x \in {\mathbb{R}}`$, $`k \in {\mathbb{N}}`$ that

#### 3.2.1 ANN approximations for one-dimensional products

###### Proposition 3.5.

Let $`\varepsilon \in {(0,1\rbrack}`$, $`q \in {(2,\infty)}`$, $`a \in {C\hspace{0pt}{({\mathbb{R}},{\mathbb{R}})}}`$ satisfy for all $`x \in {\mathbb{R}}`$ that $`{a\hspace{0pt}{(x)}} = {\max{\{ x,0\}}}`$. Then there exists $`\Phi \in \mathbf{N}`$ such that

    it holds for all $`{x,y} \in {\mathbb{R}}`$ that

```math
 & \left| {{{\lbrack{\mathcal{R}_{a}\hspace{0pt}{(\Phi)}}\rbrack}\hspace{0pt}{(x,y)}} - {x\hspace{0pt}y}} \right| \\
 & {= \left| {{\frac{1}{2}\hspace{0pt}\left\lbrack {{{\lbrack{\mathcal{R}_{a}\hspace{0pt}{(\Psi)}}\rbrack}\hspace{0pt}{({x + y})}} - {|{x + y}|}^{2}} \right\rbrack} - {\frac{1}{2}\hspace{0pt}\left\lbrack {{{\lbrack{\mathcal{R}_{a}\hspace{0pt}{(\Psi)}}\rbrack}\hspace{0pt}{(x)}} - {|x|}^{2}} \right\rbrack} - {\frac{1}{2}\hspace{0pt}\left\lbrack {{{\lbrack{\mathcal{R}_{a}\hspace{0pt}{(\Psi)}}\rbrack}\hspace{0pt}{(y)}} - {|y|}^{2}} \right\rbrack}} \right|} \\
 & {\leq {\frac{1}{2}\hspace{0pt}\left| {{{\lbrack{\mathcal{R}_{a}\hspace{0pt}{(\Psi)}}\rbrack}\hspace{0pt}{({x + y})}} - {{|{x + y}|}^{2}\hspace{0pt}\left| {+ \frac{1}{2}} \right|\hspace{0pt}{\lbrack{\mathcal{R}_{a}\hspace{0pt}{(\Psi)}}\rbrack}\hspace{0pt}{(x)}} - {{|x|}^{2}\hspace{0pt}\left| {+ \frac{1}{2}} \right|\hspace{0pt}{\lbrack{\mathcal{R}_{a}\hspace{0pt}{(\Psi)}}\rbrack}\hspace{0pt}{(y)}} - {|y|}^{2}} \right|}} \\
 & {{\leq {\frac{\delta}{2}\hspace{0pt}\left\lbrack {{\max\left\{ 1,{|{x + y}|}^{q} \right\}} + {\max\left\{ 1,{|x|}^{q} \right\}} + {\max\left\{ 1,{|y|}^{q} \right\}}} \right\rbrack}}.}
\end{array}
```

This, the fact that for all $`{\alpha,\beta} \in {\mathbb{R}}`$, $`p \in {\lbrack 1,\infty)}`$ it holds that $`{|{\alpha + \beta}|}^{p} \leq {2^{p - 1}\hspace{0pt}{({{|\alpha|}^{p} + {|\beta|}^{p}})}}`$, and the fact that $`\delta = {\varepsilon\hspace{0pt}{({2^{q - 1} + 1})}^{- 1}}`$ establish that for all $`{x,y} \in {\mathbb{R}}`$ it holds that

```math
\begin{array}{cl}
 & \left| {{{\lbrack{\mathcal{R}_{a}\hspace{0pt}{(\Phi)}}\rbrack}\hspace{0pt}{(x,y)}} - {x\hspace{0pt}y}} \right| \\
 & {\leq {\frac{\delta}{2}\hspace{0pt}\left\lbrack {{\max\left\{ 1,{{2^{q - 1}\hspace{0pt}{|x|}^{q}} + {2^{q - 1}\hspace{0pt}{|y|}^{q}}} \right\}} + {\max\left\{ 1,{|x|}^{q} \right\}} + {\max\left\{ 1,{|y|}^{q} \right\}}} \right\rbrack}} \\
 & {\leq {\frac{\delta}{2}\hspace{0pt}\left\lbrack {{\max\left\{ 1,{2^{q - 1}\hspace{0pt}{|x|}^{q}} \right\}} + {2^{q - 1}\hspace{0pt}{|y|}^{q}} + {\max\left\{ 1,{|x|}^{q} \right\}} + {\max\left\{ 1,{|y|}^{q} \right\}}} \right\rbrack}} \\
 & {{\leq {\frac{\delta}{2}\hspace{0pt}\left\lbrack {2^{q} + 2} \right\rbrack\hspace{0pt}{\max\left\{ 1,{|x|}^{q},{|y|}^{q} \right\}}} = {\varepsilon\hspace{0pt}{\max\left\{ 1,{|x|}^{q},{|y|}^{q} \right\}}}}.}
\end{array}
```

Moreover, observe that (III), (298), the triangle inequality, the fact that for all $`{\alpha,\beta} \in {\mathbb{R}}`$ it holds that $`{|{\alpha + \beta}|}^{2} \leq {2\hspace{0pt}{({{|\alpha|}^{2} + {|\beta|}^{2}})}}`$, and the fact that $`\delta = {\varepsilon\hspace{0pt}{({2^{q - 1} + 1})}^{- 1}}`$ prove that for all $`{x,y} \in {\mathbb{R}}`$ it holds that

```math
\begin{array}{cl}
{|{{\lbrack{\mathcal{R}_{a}\hspace{0pt}{(\Phi)}}\rbrack}\hspace{0pt}{(x,y)}}|} & {\leq {{\frac{1}{2}\hspace{0pt}{|{{\lbrack{\mathcal{R}_{a}\hspace{0pt}{(\Psi)}}\rbrack}\hspace{0pt}{({x + y})}}|}} + {\frac{1}{2}\hspace{0pt}{|{{\lbrack{\mathcal{R}_{a}\hspace{0pt}{(\Psi)}}\rbrack}\hspace{0pt}{(x)}}|}} + {\frac{1}{2}\hspace{0pt}{|{{\lbrack{\mathcal{R}_{a}\hspace{0pt}{(\Psi)}}\rbrack}\hspace{0pt}{(y)}}|}}}} \\
 & {\leq {{\frac{1}{2}\hspace{0pt}\left( {\delta + {|{x + y}|}^{2}} \right)} + {\frac{1}{2}\hspace{0pt}\left( {\delta + {|x|}^{2}} \right)} + {\frac{1}{2}\hspace{0pt}\left( {\delta + {|y|}^{2}} \right)}}} \\
 & {\leq {\frac{3\hspace{0pt}\delta}{2} + {\frac{3}{2}\hspace{0pt}\left( {{|x|}^{2} + {|y|}^{2}} \right)}} = {{\left\lbrack \frac{3\hspace{0pt}\varepsilon}{2} \right\rbrack\hspace{0pt}{\lbrack{2^{q - 1} + 1}\rbrack}^{- 1}} + {\frac{3}{2}\hspace{0pt}\left( {{|x|}^{2} + {|y|}^{2}} \right)}}} \\
 & {{= {\frac{3}{2}\hspace{0pt}\left\lbrack {\frac{\varepsilon}{({2^{q - 1} + 1})} + {|x|}^{2} + {|y|}^{2}} \right\rbrack} \leq {\frac{3}{2}\hspace{0pt}\left\lbrack {\frac{\varepsilon}{3} + {|x|}^{2} + {|y|}^{2}} \right\rbrack}}.}
\end{array}
```

Next note that (I) and (298) prove that for all $`{x,y} \in {\mathbb{R}}`$ it holds that

```math
\begin{array}{cl}
{{\lbrack{\mathcal{R}_{a}\hspace{0pt}{(\Phi)}}\rbrack}\hspace{0pt}{(x,0)}} & {= {{\frac{1}{2}\hspace{0pt}{\lbrack{\mathcal{R}_{a}\hspace{0pt}{(\Psi)}}\rbrack}\hspace{0pt}{(x)}} - {\frac{1}{2}\hspace{0pt}{\lbrack{\mathcal{R}_{a}\hspace{0pt}{(\Psi)}}\rbrack}\hspace{0pt}{(x)}} - {\frac{1}{2}\hspace{0pt}{\lbrack{\mathcal{R}_{a}\hspace{0pt}{(\Psi)}}\rbrack}\hspace{0pt}{(0)}}} = 0} \\
 & {{= {{\frac{1}{2}\hspace{0pt}{\lbrack{\mathcal{R}_{a}\hspace{0pt}{(\Psi)}}\rbrack}\hspace{0pt}{(y)}} - {\frac{1}{2}\hspace{0pt}{\lbrack{\mathcal{R}_{a}\hspace{0pt}{(\Psi)}}\rbrack}\hspace{0pt}{(0)}} - {\frac{1}{2}\hspace{0pt}{\lbrack{\mathcal{R}_{a}\hspace{0pt}{(\Psi)}}\rbrack}\hspace{0pt}{(y)}}} = {{\lbrack{\mathcal{R}_{a}\hspace{0pt}{(\Phi)}}\rbrack}\hspace{0pt}{(0,y)}}}.}
\end{array}
```

Furthermore, observe that the fact that $`\delta = {\varepsilon\hspace{0pt}{({2^{q - 1} + 1})}^{- 1}}`$ shows that

```math
\begin{array}{cl}
 & {{{\left\lbrack \frac{q}{2\hspace{0pt}{({q - 2})}} \right\rbrack\hspace{0pt}{\log_{2}{(\delta^{- 1})}}} + \frac{1}{({q - 2})}} = {{\left\lbrack \frac{q}{2\hspace{0pt}{({q - 2})}} \right\rbrack\hspace{0pt}{\log_{2}\left( {\varepsilon^{- 1}\hspace{0pt}{({2^{q - 1} + 1})}} \right)}} + \frac{1}{({q - 2})}}} \\
 & {= {{\frac{q}{2\hspace{0pt}{({q - 2})}}\hspace{0pt}\left\lbrack {{\log_{2}{(\varepsilon^{- 1})}} + {\log_{2}{({2^{q - 1} + 1})}}} \right\rbrack} + \frac{1}{({q - 2})}}} \\
 & {{= {{\left\lbrack \frac{q}{2\hspace{0pt}{({q - 2})}} \right\rbrack\hspace{0pt}{\log_{2}{(\varepsilon^{- 1})}}} + {\left\lbrack \frac{q}{2\hspace{0pt}{({q - 2})}} \right\rbrack\hspace{0pt}{\log_{2}{({2^{q - 1} + 1})}}} + \frac{1}{({q - 2})}}}.}
\end{array}
```

Moreover, observe that Corollary 2.21 calculus ‣ Space-time error estimates for deep neural network approximations for differential equations") implies that $`{\mathcal{P}\hspace{0pt}\left( {\mathbf{P}_{3}\hspace{0pt}{(\Psi,\Psi,\Psi)}} \right)} \leq {9\hspace{0pt}\mathcal{P}\hspace{0pt}{(\Psi)}}`$. Items (i calculus ‣ Space-time error estimates for deep neural network approximations for differential equations"))–(ii calculus ‣ Space-time error estimates for deep neural network approximations for differential equations")) in Corollary 2.9 calculus ‣ Space-time error estimates for deep neural network approximations for differential equations"), (V), (296), and (303) hence ensure that

```math
\begin{array}{cl}
 & {{\mathcal{P}\hspace{0pt}{(\Phi)}} \leq {\left\lbrack {\max\left\{ 1,\frac{\mathcal{O}\hspace{0pt}{({\mathbb{A}}_{2})}}{\mathcal{O}\hspace{0pt}{({\mathbf{P}_{3}\hspace{0pt}{(\Psi,\Psi,\Psi)}})}} \right\}} \right\rbrack\hspace{0pt}\left\lbrack {\max\left\{ 1,\frac{{\mathcal{I}\hspace{0pt}{({\mathbb{A}}_{1})}} + 1}{{\mathcal{I}\hspace{0pt}{({\mathbf{P}_{3}\hspace{0pt}{(\Psi,\Psi,\Psi)}})}} + 1} \right\}} \right\rbrack\hspace{0pt}\mathcal{P}\hspace{0pt}\left( {\mathbf{P}_{3}\hspace{0pt}{(\Psi,\Psi,\Psi)}} \right)}} \\
 & {= {\left\lbrack {\max{\{ 1,\frac{1}{3}\}}} \right\rbrack\hspace{0pt}\left\lbrack {\max{\{ 1,\frac{3}{4}\}}} \right\rbrack\hspace{0pt}\mathcal{P}\hspace{0pt}\left( {\mathbf{P}_{3}\hspace{0pt}{(\Psi,\Psi,\Psi)}} \right)} = {\mathcal{P}\hspace{0pt}\left( {\mathbf{P}_{3}\hspace{0pt}{(\Psi,\Psi,\Psi)}} \right)}} \\
 & {\leq {9\hspace{0pt}\mathcal{P}\hspace{0pt}{(\Psi)}} \leq {9\hspace{0pt}{\max\left\{ {{{\left\lbrack \frac{40\hspace{0pt}q}{({q - 2})} \right\rbrack\hspace{0pt}{\log_{2}{(\delta^{- 1})}}} + \frac{80}{({q - 2})}} - 28},52 \right\}}}} \\
 & {= {\max\left\{ {{720\hspace{0pt}\left( {{\left\lbrack \frac{q}{2\hspace{0pt}{({q - 2})}} \right\rbrack\hspace{0pt}{\log_{2}{(\delta^{- 1})}}} + \frac{1}{({q - 2})}} \right)} - 252},468 \right\}}} \\
 & {= {\max\left\{ {{720\hspace{0pt}\left( {{\left\lbrack \frac{q}{2\hspace{0pt}{({q - 2})}} \right\rbrack\hspace{0pt}{\log_{2}{(\varepsilon^{- 1})}}} + {\left\lbrack \frac{q}{2\hspace{0pt}{({q - 2})}} \right\rbrack\hspace{0pt}{\log_{2}{({2^{q - 1} + 1})}}} + \frac{1}{({q - 2})}} \right)} - 252},468 \right\}}} \\
 & {{= {\max\left\{ {{{\frac{360\hspace{0pt}q}{({q - 2})}\hspace{0pt}\left( {{\log_{2}{(\varepsilon^{- 1})}} + {\log_{2}{({2^{q - 1} + 1})}}} \right)} + \frac{720}{({q - 2})}} - 252},468 \right\}}}.}
\end{array}
```

Next note that the fact that for all $`r \in {({- \infty},4\rbrack}`$ it holds that $`r \geq {{2\hspace{0pt}r} - 4} = {2\hspace{0pt}{({r - 2})}}`$ ensures that for all $`r \in {(2,4\rbrack}`$ it holds that $`\frac{r\hspace{0pt}{({r - 1})}}{({r - 2})} \geq \frac{r}{({r - 2})} \geq 2`$. This and the fact that for all $`r \in {\lbrack 3,\infty)}`$ it holds that $`\frac{r\hspace{0pt}{({r - 1})}}{({r - 2})} \geq {r - 1} \geq 2`$ imply that for all $`r \in {(2,\infty)}`$ it holds that $`\frac{r\hspace{0pt}{({r - 1})}}{({r - 2})} \geq 2`$. Hence, we obtain that for all $`r \in {(2,\infty)}`$ it holds that

```math
\begin{array}{cl}
{{\left\lbrack \frac{360\hspace{0pt}r}{({r - 2})} \right\rbrack\hspace{0pt}{\log_{2}{({2^{r - 1} + 1})}}} - 252} & {\geq {{\left\lbrack \frac{360\hspace{0pt}r}{({r - 2})} \right\rbrack\hspace{0pt}{\log_{2}{(2^{r - 1})}}} - 252}} \\
 & {{= {\frac{360\hspace{0pt}r\hspace{0pt}{({r - 1})}}{({r - 2})} - 252} \geq {720 - 252} = 468}.}
```

Combining this with (304) shows that

The fact that

```math
\begin{array}{cl}
{\log_{2}{({2^{q - 1} + 1})}} & {= {{{\log_{2}{({2^{q - 1} + 1})}} - {\log_{2}{(2^{q})}}} + q} = {{\log_{2}\left( \frac{2^{q - 1} + 1}{2^{q}} \right)} + q}} \\
 & {= {{\log_{2}\left( {2^{- 1} + 2^{- q}} \right)} + q} \leq {{\log_{2}\left( {2^{- 1} + 2^{- 2}} \right)} + q}} \\
 & {= {{\log_{2}\left( \frac{3}{4} \right)} + q} = {{{\log_{2}{(3)}} - 2} + q}}
```

hence proves that

```math

 & {\leq {{{\frac{360\hspace{0pt}q}{({q - 2})}\hspace{0pt}\left( {{{\log_{2}{(\varepsilon^{- 1})}} + q + {\log_{2}{(3)}}} - 2} \right)} + \frac{720}{({q - 2})}} - 252}} \\
 & {= {{\frac{360\hspace{0pt}q}{({q - 2})}\hspace{0pt}\left( {{{{\log_{2}{(\varepsilon^{- 1})}} + q + {\log_{2}{(3)}}} - 2} + \frac{2}{q}} \right)} - 252}} \\
 & {{\leq {{\frac{360\hspace{0pt}q}{({q - 2})}\hspace{0pt}\left( {{{\log_{2}{(\varepsilon^{- 1})}} + q + {\log_{2}{(3)}}} - 1} \right)} - 252}}.}
\end{array}
```

In addition, observe that item (ii calculus ‣ Space-time error estimates for deep neural network approximations for differential equations")) in Proposition 2.6 calculus ‣ Space-time error estimates for deep neural network approximations for differential equations"), (296), (VI), the fact that $`\delta = {\varepsilon\hspace{0pt}{({2^{q - 1} + 1})}^{- 1}}`$, and (303) demonstrate that

```math

 & {\leq {\max\left\{ {{\left\lbrack \frac{q}{2\hspace{0pt}{({q - 2})}} \right\rbrack\hspace{0pt}{\log_{2}{(\delta^{- 1})}}} + \frac{1}{({q - 2})} + 1},2 \right\}}} \\
 & {{\leq {\max\left\{ {{\frac{q}{2\hspace{0pt}{({q - 2})}}\hspace{0pt}\left\lbrack {{\log_{2}{(\varepsilon^{- 1})}} + {\log_{2}{({2^{q - 1} + 1})}}} \right\rbrack} + \frac{({q - 1})}{({q - 2})}},2 \right\}}}.}
\end{array}
```

Furthermore, note that the fact for all $`r \in {(2,\infty)}`$ it holds that $`\frac{r\hspace{0pt}{({r - 1})}}{({r - 2})} \geq 2`$ assures that

```math
\begin{array}{cl}
 & {{\frac{q}{2\hspace{0pt}{({q - 2})}}\hspace{0pt}\left\lbrack {{\log_{2}{(\varepsilon^{- 1})}} + {\log_{2}{({2^{q - 1} + 1})}}} \right\rbrack} + \frac{({q - 1})}{({q - 2})}} \\
 & {{\geq {{\left\lbrack \frac{q}{2\hspace{0pt}{({q - 2})}} \right\rbrack\hspace{0pt}{\log_{2}{(2^{q - 1})}}} + 1} = {\frac{q\hspace{0pt}{({q - 1})}}{2\hspace{0pt}{({q - 2})}} + 1} \geq 2}.}

```

Combining this with (309) proves that

```math
\begin{array}{cl}
{\mathcal{L}\hspace{0pt}{(\Phi)}} & {\leq {{\frac{q}{2\hspace{0pt}{({q - 2})}}\hspace{0pt}\left\lbrack {{\log_{2}{(\varepsilon^{- 1})}} + {\log_{2}{({2^{q - 1} + 1})}}} \right\rbrack} + \frac{({q - 1})}{({q - 2})}}} \\
 & {\leq {{\frac{q}{2\hspace{0pt}{({q - 2})}}\hspace{0pt}\left\lbrack {{\log_{2}{(\varepsilon^{- 1})}} + {\log_{2}{({2^{q - 1} + 2^{q - 1}})}}} \right\rbrack} + \frac{q}{({q - 2})}}} \\
 & {= {\frac{q}{({q - 2})}\hspace{0pt}\left\lbrack {\frac{\log_{2}{(\varepsilon^{- 1})}}{2} + \frac{q}{2} + 1} \right\rbrack} \leq {\frac{q}{({q - 2})}\hspace{0pt}\left\lbrack {{\log_{2}{(\varepsilon^{- 1})}} + \frac{q}{2} + \frac{q}{2}} \right\rbrack}} \\
 & {{= {\frac{q}{({q - 2})}\hspace{0pt}\left\lbrack {{\log_{2}{(\varepsilon^{- 1})}} + q} \right\rbrack}}.}
```

This, the fact that $`{\mathcal{R}_{a}\hspace{0pt}{(\Phi)}} \in {C\hspace{0pt}{({\mathbb{R}}^{2},{\mathbb{R}})}}`$, (300), (301), (302), and (308) establish items (i)–(vi). The proof of Proposition 3.5 is thus completed. ∎

#### 3.2.2 ANN approximations for multi-dimensional products

###### Definition 3.6 (The Euclidean norm).

We denote by $`\left. \parallel \cdot \parallel:{( \cup_{d \in {\mathbb{N}}}{\mathbb{R}}^{d})}\rightarrow{\lbrack 0,\infty)} \right.`$ the function which satisfies for all $`d \in {\mathbb{N}}`$, $`x = {(x_{1},\ldots,x_{d})} \in {\mathbb{R}}^{d}`$ that

```math
{\left\| x \right\| = \left\lbrack {\sum_{j = 1}^{d}{|x_{j}|}^{2}} \right\rbrack^{1/2}}.
```

###### Proposition 3.7.

Let $`\varepsilon \in {(0,1\rbrack}`$, $`q \in {(2,\infty)}`$, $`d \in {\mathbb{N}}`$, $`a \in {C\hspace{0pt}{({\mathbb{R}},{\mathbb{R}})}}`$ satisfy for all $`x \in {\mathbb{R}}`$ that $`{a\hspace{0pt}{(x)}} = {\max{\{ x,0\}}}`$. Then there exists $`\Phi \in \mathbf{N}`$ such that
